{}+xA_{\Gamma}\Big(x,y,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}\Big)\Big]\,,\end{multlined}

(3.43)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S):=\tilde{\Phi}_{M,\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S)+\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)aA_{\Gamma}\Big(a,0,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}\Big)\,,

where in the last line we used that
B_{\Gamma}(0,\vartheta/\tau_{q})=0
and where in the new variables we have

\displaystyle\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)B_{\Gamma}(y,\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)})=\begin{multlined}B_{0}(y,\vartheta)+(1-\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta))B_{2}(y,\vartheta)\\
{}+\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)\sum_{j\geq 2}(\frac{1}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}-1)^{j}B_{2j}(y,\vartheta)\,,\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)A_{\Gamma}\Big(x,y,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}\Big)=\begin{multlined}(\sqrt{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta))\Sigma\ell(y,\vartheta)+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}\\
{}+\frac{\gamma q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}(\Sigma^{2}-A)\mu(y,\vartheta)\,,\end{multlined}

where we used (
6.44
); homogeneous terms of order
j
in
\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}
have weights
\sqrt{\gamma}^{j}
. We also set

(3.48)

\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta):=\Phi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma,\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}S,\gamma|\theta|^{2/3}A,\theta)\,,

and from (
3.15
),

(3.49)

\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)=|\theta|\Big(t\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}+\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,\vartheta,A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S)\Big)\\
-\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3/2}|\theta|NA^{3/2}+NhB_{L}((|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}).

The phase function
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
defines a Lagrangian
\Lambda_{N}
and, as in the model case,
we obtain a parametrization of
\pi_{N}(\Lambda_{N})
by
(d+1)
parameters
(\rho=|\theta|,\vartheta,\Sigma,A)
as follows

\displaystyle\,\,\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{t\gamma q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}+\partial_{A}\Big(\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,\vartheta,A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S)\Big)=2N\gamma^{3/2}A^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}{B_{L}}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})),\\
\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{\gamma}\Big(q^{1/3}(\vartheta)+\frac{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)})\Big)\Big)=A,\\
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\Big(q^{1/3}(\vartheta)+\frac{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\partial_{S}\Big(A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta)}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)})\Big)\Big)=A,\\
t\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}+\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,\vartheta,A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S)=\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3/2}NA^{3/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})),\\
\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\Big(t\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}+\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,\vartheta,A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,\vartheta,A,S)\Big)=0,\quad\forall 1\leq j\leq d-2,\end{array}\right.

where
{\vartheta}=({\vartheta}_{1},...,{\vartheta}_{d-1})\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
and, as
t\neq 0
implies
|y|\neq 0
, we assumed that
y_{d-1}\neq 0
; then
{\vartheta}_{d-1}=\pm\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{d-2}{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}}
. The last line in the system (
3.1
) reads as follows

(3.55)

\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}-\partial_{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}\frac{{\vartheta}_{j}}{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}=0,\quad j\in\{1,...,d-2\}.

With
\tilde{\Sigma}:=\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta})}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,{\vartheta})}
,
\frac{d\tilde{\Sigma}}{d\Sigma}=\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta})}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,{\vartheta})}
the second equation in the system (
3.1
) reads as

\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\Big(1+\partial_{\tilde{\Sigma}}\Big(A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\tilde{\Sigma},\frac{\vartheta}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)})\Big)\Big)=A.

We further compute, with
\tau_{q}=\tau_{q}(\gamma A,{\vartheta})=\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta})}
,

(3.56)

\quad\partial_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,{\vartheta},A,\Sigma)=\partial_{A}\tau_{q}\Big(\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau_{q})^{k}}{\tau_{q}^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,\vartheta)-B_{2}(y,\vartheta)\Big)\\
{}+\gamma^{3/2}\Big(-\Sigma+\frac{\partial_{A}\tau_{q}}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\frac{x}{\gamma}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta)/\tau_{q},\vartheta/\tau_{q})\Big)\Big)\,,

where the last term in the second line is small and behaves like
xO(\sqrt{\gamma})
; indeed, it follows from (
3.1
) that
\partial_{\tau_{q}}(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma})=-\gamma q^{2/3}({\vartheta})\frac{(\Sigma^{2}-A)}{\tau_{q}^{2}}\mu(y,{\vartheta})+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}
, hence its main contribution is
O(\gamma)
(here we have also used the second and the third equations in (
3.1
) which imply that
\Sigma^{2},S^{2}\leq 2A
where the phase may be stationary in
\Sigma,S
). Using that for
k\geq 2
, all terms in
B_{\Gamma}
come with factors
O(\gamma^{2})
and
\partial_{A}\tau_{q}=\gamma q^{2/3}({\vartheta})/(2\tau_{q})
, the main contribution in the first equation in (
3.1
) reads

(3.57)

\frac{q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,\vartheta))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-\Sigma+S+O(\sqrt{\gamma}x)+O(\gamma^{3/2}|y|)=2NA^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}{B_{L}}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})).

We now turn to critical points with respect to
\vartheta
and deal with the last equation in (
3.1
).

Lemma 3.8
.

The last equation in the system (
3.1
) has two solutions, denoted
{\vartheta}_{\pm}
, such that

(3.58)

\pm{\vartheta}_{\pm}=\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})}{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})|}-\frac{\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta}_{\pm})}{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})|}\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q({\vartheta}_{\pm})}{3q({\vartheta}_{\pm})}\Big(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})\Big]\\
{}-\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})|}\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q({\vartheta}_{\pm})}{3q^{2/3}({\vartheta}_{\pm})}\Big(\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm}))-\frac{a}{\gamma}S\Big)+\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta}_{\pm})\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}(\ell(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm}))\Big]+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y|}\mathcal{E}_{\pm},

with
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
smooth functions. Moreover,
\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})}{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{\pm})|}\Big)\cdot{\vartheta}_{\pm}=\pm 1+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\mathcal{E}_{\pm}
.

Proof.

Using (
3.55
) with
{\vartheta}^{2}_{d-1}=1-\sum_{j=1}^{d-2}{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}
,
\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d-2}|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}\Big){\vartheta}_{d-1}^{2}=(1-{\vartheta}_{d-1}^{2})|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}
. We eventually obtain

(3.59)

{\vartheta}_{d-1}=\pm\frac{\partial_{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}}{|(\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}|^{1/2}},\quad{\vartheta}_{j}={\vartheta}_{d-1}\frac{\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}}{\partial_{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}}=\pm\frac{\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}}{|\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}|^{1/2}}.

For each sign
\pm
there exists an unique solution as the maps from
\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
to
\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
:
{\vartheta}\rightarrow\pm\frac{\nabla_{{\vartheta}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}}{|\nabla_{{\vartheta}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|}
each have a unique fixed point for small
|y|,t
(as
B_{0}=O(|y|^{2})
,
B_{2}=O(|y|^{2})
and
\gamma
is small).

In the following we approximate the equation satisfied by
{\vartheta}
up to
O(\gamma^{2})
terms. This will turn out to be useful later on, in the proof of Proposition
3.13
. Using (
3.49
) with
N=0
together with (
3.1
),(
3.1
) and (
3.1
), we write

(3.60)

\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho{\vartheta})=\rho\Big(t\tau_{q}+\tilde{\Phi}_{M,\gamma}(x,y,{\vartheta},A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{M,\gamma}(a,0,{\vartheta},A,S)\\
+B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})+(1-\tau_{q})B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{3/2}\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta})\Sigma\ell(y,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(x,a,y,{\vartheta},\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma,\sqrt{\gamma}S,A)\Big),

where we have set, for
\tau_{q}=\tau_{q}(\gamma A,{\vartheta})
,

(3.61)

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}:=\gamma^{-2}\Big(\tau_{q}B_{\Gamma}(y,{\vartheta}/\tau_{q})-B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})-(1-\tau_{q})B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})+x(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}-\sqrt{\gamma}xq^{1/3}({\vartheta})\ell(y,{\vartheta})\Sigma)-a\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}S\Big).

From (
3.1
) and (
3.1
),
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}
is a smooth bounded function and

(3.62)

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}=\frac{(1-\tau_{q})^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\tau_{q}}\Big(B_{4}(y,{\vartheta})+\sum_{j\geq 3}(\frac{1}{\tau_{q}}-1)^{j-2}B_{2j}(y,{\vartheta})\Big)+\frac{x}{\gamma}\Big(q^{2/3}({\vartheta})(\Sigma^{2}-A)\mu(y,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}\Big)\\
-\frac{a}{\gamma}\Big(q^{2/3}({\vartheta})(S^{2}-A)\mu(0,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}\Big),

where homogeneous terms of order
j
in
\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}
come from
A_{\Gamma}
and have factors
\gamma^{j/2-1}
,
j\geq 3
and where
(1-\tau_{q})^{2}/\gamma^{2}=A^{2}q^{4/3}({\vartheta})/(1+\tau_{q})^{2}
is bounded. We compute explicitly

(3.63)

\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}=y_{j}+\partial_{j}B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})+\frac{\gamma A}{2\tau_{q}}q^{2/3}({\vartheta})\Big[\frac{2\partial_{j}q({\vartheta})}{3q({\vartheta})}(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}))-\frac{2}{1+\tau_{q}}\partial_{j}B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})\Big]\\
+\gamma^{3/2}\Big[\frac{\partial_{j}q({\vartheta})}{3q^{2/3}({\vartheta})}\Big(\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}))-\frac{a}{\gamma}S\Big)+\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta})\partial_{j}\ell(y,{\vartheta})\Big]+\gamma^{2}\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}.

With
\nabla F=(\partial_{{\vartheta}_{1}}F,...,\partial_{{\vartheta}_{d-1}}F)
, let
\Omega_{1}=\frac{A}{2\tau_{q}}q^{2/3}({\vartheta})\Big(\frac{2\nabla q({\vartheta})}{3q({\vartheta})}(1-\frac{B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})}{t})-\frac{2}{1+\tau_{q}}\frac{\nabla B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})}{t}\Big)
,
\Omega_{2}=\frac{\nabla q({\vartheta})}{3q^{2/3}({\vartheta})}\Big(\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}))-\frac{a}{\gamma}S\Big)+\frac{x}{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta})\nabla\ell(y,{\vartheta})
, then, using (
3.63
) we compute,

(3.64)

\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}=\Big|y+\nabla B_{0}+\gamma t\Omega_{1}+\gamma^{3/2}\Omega_{2}+\gamma^{2}\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}\Big|^{2}=|y+\nabla B_{0}|^{2}\Big[1+\frac{2\gamma t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\times\\
{}\Omega_{1}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)+\frac{2\gamma^{3/2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Omega_{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big(\mathcal{E}_{1}+\frac{t^{2}\mathcal{E}_{2}+t\sqrt{\gamma}\mathcal{E}_{3}+\gamma\mathcal{E}_{4}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)\Big],

where we have set
\mathcal{E}_{1}:=2\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}:=|\Omega_{1}|^{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{3}=2\Omega_{1}\cdot(\Omega_{2}+\sqrt{\gamma}\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma})
,
\mathcal{E}_{4}=|\Omega_{2}|^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma}\Omega_{2}\cdot(\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma})+\gamma|\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}|^{2}
. As
\frac{\gamma}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}=\sqrt{\gamma}\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t}\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}
is small when
t/\sqrt{\gamma}
is sufficiently large, the coefficient of
\mathcal{E}_{3}
in (
3.64
) is bounded by
O(\sqrt{\gamma})
. All the three terms
\mathcal{E}_{j}
are smooth, bounded functions and we relabel their sum as follows

\mathcal{E}_{1}+\frac{t^{2}\mathcal{E}_{2}+t\sqrt{\gamma}\mathcal{E}_{3}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}+\frac{\gamma}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\mathcal{E}_{4}=\mathcal{E}(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta}).

We eventually find

(3.65)

\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}|\partial_{{\vartheta}_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}=|y+\nabla B_{0}|\Big(1+\frac{\gamma t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}<\Omega_{1},\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}>\\
+\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}<\Omega_{2},\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}>+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\mathcal{\tilde{E}}\Big),

where
\mathcal{\tilde{E}}
is a smooth function of
(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
obtained by taking the square root in (
3.64
) and using the asymptotic expansion of
\sqrt{1+\kappa}=1+\kappa/2+O(\kappa^{2})
.
From (
3.59
) we have

\pm{\vartheta}=\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}+\gamma t\Omega_{1}+\gamma^{3/2}\Omega_{2}+\gamma^{2}\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|\Big(1+\frac{\gamma t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Omega_{1}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Omega_{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\mathcal{\tilde{R}}\Big)}.

Setting
\tilde{\Omega}_{j}=\frac{\Omega_{j}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}
and using
(1+\kappa)^{-1}=1-\kappa+O(\kappa^{2})
, we obtain, for another smooth and bounded function
\mathcal{\breve{E}}(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
,

(3.66)

\pm{\vartheta}=\Big\{\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}+\gamma t\tilde{\Omega}_{1}+\gamma^{3/2}\tilde{\Omega}_{2}+\gamma^{2}\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}/|y+\nabla B_{0}|\Big\}\\
\times\Big\{1-\gamma t\tilde{\Omega}_{1}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)-\gamma^{3/2}\tilde{\Omega}_{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\breve{R}\Big\}\\
=\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}+\frac{\gamma t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big(\Omega_{1}-\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Omega_{1}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)\Big)\\
+\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big(\Omega_{2}-\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Omega_{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\Big)\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}\mathcal{E},

and replacing
\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|}
in the last two lines by
\pm{\vartheta}-\gamma t\tilde{\Omega}_{1}-\gamma^{3/2}\tilde{\Omega}_{2}-\gamma^{2}\nabla\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}/|y+\nabla B_{0}|
yields (
3.58
) where
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}
are smooth, bounded functions of
(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

For
t>0
, considering the critical point satisfying (
3.58
) with
+
sign in the left hand side term produces
O(h^{\infty})
contributions, as the critical value of the phase is not stationary in
\rho
. Therefore in the following for
t>0
we pick
{\vartheta}_{c}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A):={\vartheta}_{-}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A)
such that (
3.58
) holds (and for
t<0
one should take
{\vartheta}_{c}:={\vartheta}_{+}
).

In Lemma
3.8
we only considered critical points of
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
with respect to
{\vartheta}
. In the next Lemma we deal with critical points with respect to
A
,
\rho
and
{\vartheta}
of
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
, where
\rho=|\theta|
,
\theta=\rho{\vartheta}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
t>0
. If
\nabla_{A,\rho,{\vartheta}}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}=0
then there exists a smooth, bounded function
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
depending on
(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
such that for the critical point with respect to
{\vartheta}
, that we denote
{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp}
(and for which (
3.58
) holds with
{\vartheta}_{-}
)

(3.67)

\frac{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})|}{t}=1+\frac{1}{6}\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})\Big(1-\frac{B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})}{t}\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t}\Big(\frac{2}{3}A(\Sigma-S)\\
{}+\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}^{\sharp}_{c}))-A)-\frac{S^{3}}{3}-S(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})-A)\Big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{t}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\,.

Moreover, the critical point
{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp}
is such that

(3.68)

-{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp}=\frac{y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})}{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})|}-2\Big(\frac{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})|}{t}-1\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q({\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})}{q({\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})}-\frac{3\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}_{c}^{\sharp})}{2t(1-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}^{\sharp}_{c})/t)}\Big]+O(\frac{\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}}{t})\,.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.8
, (
3.58
) holds (critical point w.r.t.
{\vartheta}
). Let
\partial_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}=\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}=0
(first and
second to last equations in (
3.1
)): substitution between the two equations yields

(3.69)

t\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta})}+\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,{\vartheta},A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,{\vartheta},A,S)\\
=\frac{2}{3}A\Big(\frac{t\gamma q^{2/3}({\vartheta})}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta})}}+\partial_{A}\Big(\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(x,y,{\vartheta},A,\Sigma)-\tilde{\Phi}_{\gamma}(a,0,{\vartheta},A,S)\Big)\Big),

and using (
3.1
) and (
3.56
) we further obtain, with
{\tau_{q}=\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta)}
,
\partial_{A}\tau_{q}=\gamma q^{2/3}({\vartheta})/(2\tau_{q})
,

(3.70)

t\tau_{q}+y\cdot{\vartheta}+\gamma^{3/2}\Big(\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)-A)-\frac{S^{3}}{3}-S(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)-A)\Big)+B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})\\
{}+(1-\tau_{q})B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{3/2}\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta})\Sigma\ell(y,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}=\frac{2}{3}\frac{\gamma Aq^{2/3}}{2\tau_{q}}\Big[t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau_{q})^{k}}{\tau_{q}^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta})\\
{}+x\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta})/\tau_{q},{\vartheta}/\tau_{q})\Big)-a\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\sqrt{\gamma}Sq^{1/3}({\vartheta})/\tau_{q},{\vartheta}/\tau_{q})\Big)\Big]-\frac{2}{3}\gamma^{3/2}(\Sigma-S)A

with
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}
defined in (
3.61
). As, from (
3.1
), we obtain
\partial_{\tau_{q}}(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma})=-\gamma q^{2/3}({\vartheta})(\Sigma^{2}-A)\mu(y,{\vartheta})/\tau_{q}^{2}+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}
, we define a smooth, bounded function
\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma}

(3.71)

\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{3}\frac{Aq^{1/3}({\vartheta})}{\tau_{q}}\Big[\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau_{q})^{k}}{\gamma\tau_{q}^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta})\\
+\frac{x}{\gamma}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta})/\tau_{q},{\vartheta}/\tau_{q})\Big)-\frac{a}{\gamma}\partial_{\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q}A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\sqrt{\gamma}Sq^{1/3}({\vartheta})/\tau_{q},{\vartheta}/\tau_{q})\Big].

Note that
\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma}=\frac{Aq({\vartheta})}{3\tau_{q}^{2}}(\frac{2A}{(1+\tau_{q})}-x(\Sigma^{2}-A)\mu(y,{\vartheta})+a(S^{2}-A)\gamma(0,{\vartheta})+\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3})
, where terms in
\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 3}
come with wights
\gamma^{j/2}
and
j\geq 3
. Equation (
3.70
) becomes

(3.72)

t+y\cdot{\vartheta}+B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})=-\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta})(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}))(\frac{1}{1+\tau_{q}}-\frac{1}{3\tau_{q}})-\gamma^{2}(\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}-\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma})\\
{}-\gamma^{3/2}\Big(\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}))-A)-\frac{S^{3}}{3}-S(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)-A)+\frac{2}{3}(\Sigma-S)A\Big)\,.

As
\tau_{q}^{2}-1=\gamma Aq^{2/3}/(1+\tau_{q})
and
(\frac{1}{1+\tau_{q}}-\frac{1}{3\tau_{q}})=\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{12}\frac{(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)(1-(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/4)}{\tau_{q}^{2}(1+(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/2)}
, we move the part of the coefficient of
(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}))
with factor
\gamma^{2}
into the next term. Recall
B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})={\vartheta}\cdot\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta})
, which eventually yields

(3.73)

(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}))\cdot{\vartheta}+t=-\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta})(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta}))\Big(\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{12}\frac{(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)(1-(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/4)}{\tau_{q}^{2}(1+(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/2)}\Big)\\
{}-\gamma^{3/2}\Big(\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta}))-A)-\frac{S^{3}}{3}-S(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)-A)+\frac{2}{3}(\Sigma-S)A\Big)+\gamma^{2}(\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}-\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma})\,.

As
{\vartheta}
is itself a critical point, we obtain, using the last statement of Lemma
3.8
and Remark
3.9
, that (
3.58
) holds with
-
sign on the left, and that

\vartheta\cdot(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta}))=-|y+\nabla B_{0}|+\gamma^{2}\mathcal{E}_{-}
. As a result we obtain (
3.67
) with
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}=\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(t,x,y,\frac{t}{|y+\nabla B_{0}|},\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{t},{\vartheta})
, defined as follows

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}:=\tilde{\Gamma}_{\gamma}-\breve{\Gamma}_{\gamma}+\mathcal{E}_{-}+\frac{1}{12}\frac{A^{2}q^{4/3}({\vartheta}v)}{1+\tau_{q}}\frac{(1-(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/4)}{\tau_{q}^{2}(1+(\tau_{q}^{2}-1)/2)}(t-B_{2}(y,\vartheta)\,,

which completes the proof of Lemma
3.10
.
∎

Pick
(t,x,y)
with
t>0
and
\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)\neq\varnothing
. As already noticed, we must have
||y|/t-1|\leq c
(since otherwise
\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)=\emptyset
by (
3.67
)). Let now
N_{j}\in\mathcal{N}^{1}_{d}(t,x,y)
,
j\in\{1,2\}
. Then there exist
(t_{j},x_{j},y_{j})
such that
N_{j}\in\mathcal{N}(t_{j},x_{j},y_{j})
and
(t_{j},x_{j},y_{j})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
; there exist
(\theta_{j},A_{j},\Sigma_{j},S_{j})
,
j\in\{1,2\}
,
A_{j}
close to
1

such that (
3.1
) holds with
(t,x,y,\theta,A,\Sigma,S)
replaced by
(t_{j},x_{j},y_{j},\theta_{j},A_{j},\Sigma_{j},S_{j})
. Then we have, with
\vartheta_{j}=\theta_{j}/|\theta_{j}|
,

(3.74)

\displaystyle\quad 2N_{j}(1-\frac{3}{4}{B_{L}}^{\prime}(|\theta_{j}|\lambda_{\gamma}A_{j}^{3/2}))=\frac{q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{j})}{2A_{j}^{1/2}\sqrt{1+\gamma A_{j}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{j})}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big(t_{j}-B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})\Big)+O(1)\,,

(3.75)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j}^{2}+\frac{x_{j}}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{j})\Big(1+\ell(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 1}\Big)=A_{j},S_{j}^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{j})(1+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 1})=A_{j}\,,

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}\frac{y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{t_{j}}=-\vartheta_{j}+\gamma A_{j}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{j})\Big[(1-\frac{B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{t_{j}})\Big(\frac{1}{2}\vartheta_{j}-\frac{\nabla q(\vartheta_{j})}{3q(\vartheta_{j})}\Big)\\
{}+\frac{1}{2t_{j}}\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})\Big]+O(\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{j}})\,.\end{multlined}

Taking the difference between (
3.74
) for
j=1,2
yields

(3.78)

N_{1}-N_{2}=\frac{3}{4}\Big(N_{1}B_{L}^{\prime}(|\theta_{1}|\lambda_{\gamma}A_{1}^{3/2})-N_{2}B_{L}^{\prime}(|\theta_{2}|\lambda_{\gamma}A_{2}^{3/2})\Big)+O(1)\\
+\frac{(t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1}))q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{1})}{4\sqrt{\gamma}A_{1}^{1/2}\sqrt{1+\gamma A_{1}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{1})}}-\frac{(t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2}))q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{2})}{4\sqrt{\gamma}A_{2}^{1/2}\sqrt{1+\gamma A_{2}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{2})}}.

Using Remark
3.6
, the first term in the RHS of (
3.78
) is
O\Big(\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}}\Big)
. We are reduced to proving

(3.79)

\Big|\frac{(t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1}))q(\vartheta_{1})}{\sqrt{A_{1}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{1})}}-\frac{(t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2}))q(\vartheta_{2})}{\sqrt{A_{2}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{2})}}\Big|=O(\sqrt{\gamma}).

This will follow from the next lemma :

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
(t,x,y)
be fixed, let
(t_{j},x_{j},y_{j})
satisfy (
3.18
), (
3.19
) and let
(\theta_{j}=|\theta_{j}|\vartheta_{j},A_{j},\Sigma_{j},S_{j})
,
j\in\{1,2\}
with
A_{j}
close to
1
such that (
3.74
), (
3.75
) and (
3.1
) hold true, then

(3.80)

t|\vartheta_{1}-\vartheta_{2}|\lesssim\sqrt{\gamma},\quad t|A_{1}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{1})-A_{2}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{2})|\lesssim\sqrt{\gamma}.

Proof.

If
\frac{t}{\sqrt{\gamma}}
is bounded then both inequalities in (
3.80
) follow immediately. Suppose
\frac{t}{\sqrt{\gamma}}
is sufficiently large.
From (
3.1
) and (
3.58
) we get (as in (
3.67
))

(3.81)

\displaystyle\frac{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})|-t_{j}}{t_{j}-B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\gamma A_{j}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{j})+O(\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{j}})\,.

\displaystyle\frac{y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})|}+\vartheta_{j}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}-\gamma A_{j}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{j})\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(\vartheta_{j})}{3q(\vartheta_{j})}\Big(1-\frac{B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{t_{j}}\Big)\\
{}-\frac{1}{2t_{j}}\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})\Big]+O(\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{j}})\,.\end{multlined}

Taking the difference between (
3.1
) for
j=1
and
j=2
yields

|\vartheta_{1}-\vartheta_{2}|=\Big|\frac{y_{1}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}{|y_{1}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})|}-\frac{y_{2}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}{|y_{2}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})|}\Big|+O(\gamma).

As
\nabla B_{0}(y,\vartheta)=O(|y|^{2})
,
\Big|\frac{y_{1}}{|y_{1}|}-\frac{y_{2}}{|y_{2}|}\Big|\leq\frac{2r_{0}\sqrt{\gamma}}{|y|}
from (
3.18
) and
\frac{t}{|y|}\sim 1
, the first inequality in (
3.80
) holds true. We proceed with the second one, which is, as for the model, more delicate to handle.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j}={\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j}(t_{j},y_{j})
be the solution to (
3.84
) below, then
|\vartheta_{j}-{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j}|\lesssim\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{j}}
.

(3.84)

{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j}+\frac{y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})}{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})|}=-2\Big(\frac{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})|}{t_{j}}-1\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q({\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})}{q({\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})}-\frac{3\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})}{2t_{j}(1-\frac{B_{2}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})}{t_{j}})}\Big]\,.

Proof.

The coefficient of
\gamma A_{j}
in (
3.81
) does not vanish as
B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})=O(|y_{j}|^{2})
and
|y_{j}|/t_{j}\sim 1
; we replace
\gamma A_{j}
in (
3.1
) by its first approximation given in (
3.81
). We obtain

(3.85)

\vartheta_{j}=-\frac{y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})|}\\
-2\Big(\frac{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})|}{t_{j}}-1\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(\vartheta_{j})}{q(\vartheta_{j})}-\frac{3\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{2t_{j}{(1-\frac{B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta_{j})}{t_{j}})}}\Big]+O(\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{j}}).

Taking the difference between (
3.85
) and (
3.84
), using
\nabla B_{2}(y_{j},\vartheta)=O(|y_{j}|^{2})
and smallness of
\Big|\frac{|y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{j})|}{t_{j}}-1\Big|
, completes the proof of Lemma
3.12
.
∎

Using (
3.81
), in order to achieve the proof of Lemma
3.11
we are reduced to proving that

(3.86)

\frac{t}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|\frac{|y_{1}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})|-t_{1}}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}-\frac{|y_{2}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})|-t_{2}}{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}\Big|=O(1).

Set
\tilde{y}_{j}=y_{j}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{j},\vartheta_{{j}})
and write

(3.87)

\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{1}}|-t_{1}}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}-\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|-t_{2}}{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{1}}|-t_{1}+t_{2}-|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}\\
{}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad{}-(t_{2}-|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|)\Big|\frac{1}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}-\frac{1}{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}\Big|\\
\leq\Big|\frac{|y_{1}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{1},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{1})|-t_{1}+t_{2}-|y_{2}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{2},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{2})|}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}\Big|+\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{1}}|-|y_{1}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{1},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{1})|}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}\Big|\\
+\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|-|y_{2}+\nabla B_{0}(y_{2},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{2})|}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}\Big|+\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|-t_{2}}{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}\Big|\Big|\frac{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}-1\Big|

and using (
3.17
) and (
3.19
), Lemma
3.12
and (
3.81
) for
j=2
yields

(3.88)

\frac{t}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{1}}|-t_{1}}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}-\frac{|{\tilde{y}_{2}}|-t_{2}}{t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},\vartheta_{2})}\Big|\leq\frac{t}{\gamma^{3/2}}\times\frac{r_{0}\gamma^{3/2}}{t_{1}}\\
+\frac{t}{\gamma^{3/2}}\times\Big(O(\frac{|y_{1}|^{2}}{t_{1}})|\vartheta_{1}-{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{1}|+O(\frac{|y_{2}|^{2}}{t_{1}})|\vartheta_{2}-{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{2}|\Big)\\
+\frac{t}{\gamma^{3/2}}\times O(\gamma)\times\Big(\frac{|t_{2}-B_{2}(y_{2},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{2})-t_{1}+B_{2}(y_{1},{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{1})|}{t_{1}-B_{2}(y_{1},\vartheta_{1})}+O(\frac{|y_{1}|^{2}}{t_{1}})|\vartheta_{1}-{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{1}|+O(\frac{|y_{2}|^{2}}{t_{1}})|\vartheta_{2}-{\tilde{\vartheta}}_{2}|\Big)=O(1).

For
d=2
the proof is much simpler (no angle
\vartheta
). In this case Lemma
3.11
reduces to obtaining suitable estimates for
|A_{1}-A_{2}|
; this follows from the
2D
equivalent of
(
3.81
) which reads

\frac{|y_{j}+B_{0}(y_{j})|}{t_{j}}=1+\frac{1}{6}\gamma A_{j}\Big(1-\frac{B_{2}(y_{j})}{t_{j}}\Big)+O(\gamma^{3/2}/t)\,,

and we are done with Lemma
3.11
.∎

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.4
in the general case.
∎

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
(t,x,y)
such that
\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)\neq\emptyset
. Then,

\sum_{N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1}_{d}(t,x,y),|N|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}}V_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)=O(h^{\infty}).

Proof.

We, again, consider first the model case: in rescaled variables
T,X,Y
, the Lagrangian
\Lambda^{M}_{N}
is defined by (
3.23
), (
3.24
), (
3.1
).
We start with integration in
\vartheta
:
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}=\Psi^{M}_{0,a,\gamma}-\frac{4}{3}|\theta|NA^{3/2}+\frac{N}{\lambda_{\gamma}}B_{L}((|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})
and the last two terms do not depend on
\vartheta
;
\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}=\nabla_{\vartheta}^{2}\Psi^{M}_{0,a,\gamma}=\frac{|Y|}{\gamma}\times O(1)=\frac{|y|}{\gamma^{3/2}}\times O(1)
. Using Lemma
3.2
, we have
|y|>c_{0}|t|
, hence critical points with respect to
\vartheta
are non-degenerate and stationary phase applies (providing a factor
(\lambda_{\gamma}\frac{|y|}{\gamma^{3/2}})^{-(d-2)/2}\lesssim(\frac{h}{t})^{(d-2)/2}
). Let
t>0
, then using Lemma
3.8
with
\Gamma=0
(hence
B_{0}=B_{2}=l=0
), there exists a smooth, bounded function
\mathcal{E}_{M,-}
such that the critical point (associated to the
-
sign in (
3.58
)) is

(3.89)

\vartheta_{c}=-{\vartheta_{Y}}-\frac{\gamma A}{3}\frac{T}{|Y|}\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(-{\vartheta_{Y}})}{q^{1/3}(-{\vartheta_{Y}})}+\frac{\gamma}{|Y|}(\Sigma X-S\frac{a}{\gamma})\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(-{\vartheta_{Y}})}{3q^{2/3}(-{\vartheta_{Y}})}+\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{|Y|}\mathcal{E}_{M,-}\,,

where we set
\vartheta_{Y}=Y/|Y|
.
The critical point (up to its sign) is unique, and from Lemma
3.10
(in the model case) we see that we recover (
3.29
). Let
\rho:=|\theta|
and denote
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho):=\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho\vartheta_{c})
the critical value of the phase at
\vartheta_{c}
given above, then

(3.90)

\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)=\rho\Big(\frac{Y\cdot\vartheta_{c}+T\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})}}{\gamma}+\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(Xq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A)\\
-\frac{S^{3}}{3}-S(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A)-\frac{4}{3}NA^{3/2}\Big)+\frac{N}{\lambda_{\gamma}}B_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})\,.

Define sets of integers related to stationary points of this phase:

(3.91)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y):=\{N\in\mathbb{Z},\exists(\Sigma,S,A,\rho)\text{ such that }\nabla_{(\Sigma,S,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)=0\}\,,

(3.92)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y):=\cup_{\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)}\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\,.

One sees that
\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)=\mathcal{N}^{M}(t,x,y)
, which implies
\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)=\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{d}(t,x,y)
, where
(T,X,Y)=(\frac{t}{\sqrt{\gamma}},\frac{x}{\gamma},\frac{y}{\sqrt{\gamma}})
: indeed, if
N
is such that there exists a critical point
(\Sigma,S,A,\rho)
for
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
at
(T,X,Y)
, then
(\Sigma,S,A,\rho,\vartheta_{c}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,a,\gamma))
is a critical point for
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
and the converse also holds. We now need to prove that

\sum_{N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y),|N|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}}V^{M}_{N,\gamma}(T,X,Y)=O(h^{\infty}),

where
V^{M}_{N,\gamma}(T,X,Y)
had phase
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
that became
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
after the stationary phase in
\vartheta
.

Let first
4a\lesssim\gamma
: stationary phase applies in
S
. Indeed,
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
is stationary in
S
when
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})=A
and for
\frac{a}{\gamma}
small enough there are two non-degenerate critical points
S_{\pm}
(with main contributions
\pm\sqrt{A}
). We denote
by
\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}
the critical values of the phase
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
at
S_{\pm}
. For
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y):=\{N\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,\,\exists(\Sigma,A,\rho)\text{ such that }\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)=0\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y):=\cup_{\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)}\mathcal{N}^{M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\cap_{\pm}\mathcal{N}^{M,\pm}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)=\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)\,.

If
N\in\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
, then there exists a critical point
\Sigma_{c},S_{c},A_{c},\rho_{c}
for the phase
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
;
S_{c}
satisfies
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})=A
, hence
S_{c}\in\{S_{\pm}\}
. Therefore
(\Sigma_{c},A_{c},\rho_{c})
is a critical point for
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S_{\pm},A,\rho\vartheta_{c}|_{S_{\pm}})=\tilde{\Psi}^{\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}
. Conversely, let
\Sigma_{\pm},A_{\pm},\rho_{\pm}
be a critical point for
\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}
; for each sign
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, let
S_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}
denote both solutions to
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c}|_{A_{\varepsilon},\Sigma_{\varepsilon},S})=A_{\varepsilon}
, then
(\Sigma_{\varepsilon},S_{\varepsilon}^{\pm},A_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon})
are critical points for
\tilde{\Psi}^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}
and both inclusions hold. Using
\cap_{\pm}\mathcal{N}^{M,\pm}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)=\mathcal{N}^{M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
we obtain
\cap_{\pm}\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\pm}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)=\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
and therefore

(\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y))^{c}=\cup_{\pm}(\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\pm}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y))^{c}
, where
(\mathcal{N}^{1,M}_{\tilde{\Psi}})^{c}
is the complement set. Hence, the proof of Proposition
3.13
for
\Delta_{M}
(for
4a\lesssim\gamma
) rests on proving

\sum_{N\notin\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}}\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y),|N|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}}V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(T,X,Y)=O(h^{\infty}),

where now
V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}
has phase function
\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
and its symbol is obtained from the one of
V^{M}_{N,\gamma}
after stationary phase in
S
. Using (
3.90
), we obtain, with
\vartheta_{c}
satisfying (
3.29
)

(3.93)

\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)=\rho\Big(\frac{Y\cdot\vartheta_{c}+T\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})}}{\gamma}+\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(Xq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A)\\
\mp\frac{2}{3}(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A)^{3/2}-\frac{4}{3}NA^{3/2}\Big)+\frac{N}{\lambda_{\gamma}}B_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}).

Lemma 3.14
.

There exists a uniform constant
c>0
such that, if
N\notin\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}}\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
then, for all
(\Sigma,A,\rho)
on the support of the symbol of
V^{M,\pm}_{N,\gamma}(T,X,Y)
,

(3.94)

|\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\geq c.

Remark 3.15
.

The lemma allows to conclude the proof of Proposition
3.13
in the case
4a\lesssim\gamma
as, using (
3.94
), we apply non stationary phase with respect to
(\Sigma,A,\rho)
and obtain a contribution
O(h^{\infty})
for each such
V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
; the sum over
N
is finite (up to
|N|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}
) and we can sum up.

Before dealing with the proof of Lemma
3.14
, we go back to (
3.23
), (
3.24
) and (
3.1
):
we set
S=S_{\pm}(A,\vartheta)
is such that
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)=A
. For each
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, we explicitly obtain the integral curves
(T_{\varepsilon},X_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon})(A,\Sigma,\theta)

depending on the parameters
(A,\Sigma,\theta)
,

\displaystyle\,\,\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\vartheta)q^{1/3}(\vartheta)=A-\Sigma^{2},\quad S_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\sqrt{A-\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)},\\
T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,|\theta|,\vartheta)\frac{q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}=2NA^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}))+(\Sigma-S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta)),\\
\begin{multlined}Y_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,|\theta|,\vartheta)=-\frac{T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,|\theta|,\vartheta)q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}\Big(\frac{\vartheta}{q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}(1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta))+\frac{\gamma A}{3}\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(\vartheta)}{q(\vartheta)}\Big)\\
{}-\gamma\Big(\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(X_{\varepsilon}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A)-\varepsilon\frac{2}{3}S^{3}_{\varepsilon}\Big)\vartheta+\frac{4}{3}\gamma NA^{3/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}))\vartheta\\
{}-\gamma\Big(\Sigma X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\vartheta)-S_{\varepsilon}\frac{a}{\gamma}\Big)q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\frac{\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(\vartheta)}{3q(\vartheta)}\,.\end{multlined}\end{array}\right.v

Taking the scalar product with
\vartheta
in the last equation and using that
\vartheta\cdot\mathord{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.16667pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.34029pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.88199pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\nabla}}}}q(\vartheta)=0
yields

Y_{\varepsilon}\cdot\vartheta+T_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{\frac{2}{3}}(\vartheta)}+\gamma\Big(\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma(X_{\varepsilon}q^{\frac{1}{3}}(\vartheta_{c})-A)-\varepsilon\frac{2}{3}S^{3}_{\varepsilon}\Big)=\frac{4}{3}\gamma NA^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{\frac{3}{2}})),

which is nothing but the derivative of the phase with respect to
\rho=|\theta|
.

Proof.

(of (
3.94
))
Fix
(T,X,Y)
. Let
N\notin\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}}\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
, hence
N\notin\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}}\mathcal{N}^{M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
for all
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)
, which is equivalent to
\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)\neq 0
for all
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)
. Pick a sign
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, let
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)
, and let
(A,\Sigma,\rho)
be a given point (on the support of the symbol of
V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
); let
S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta_{c}^{\prime})
and
\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}
be solutions to
S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c}^{\prime})=A
and (
3.89
), where in (
3.89
) we replace
(T,X,Y)
by
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
and where the sign of
S_{\varepsilon}
is
\varepsilon
. We may compute
\nabla_{(A,\Sigma,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)
,
taking advantage of
\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}
and
S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta_{c}^{\prime})
being stationary points for
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,\gamma}(\cdots)
to cancel their derivatives. Let now
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
,
using (
3.1
) and

S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta)=\varepsilon\sqrt{A-\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)}
), we get

\displaystyle\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdot)=\frac{T^{\prime}q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}}-\frac{T_{\varepsilon}q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}+S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta),\\
\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdot)=X^{\prime}q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-X_{\varepsilon}q^{1/3}(\vartheta),\\
\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdot)=\begin{multlined}\gamma^{-1}\bigl({Y^{\prime}\cdot\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}+T^{\prime}\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{\frac{2}{3}}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}}-{Y_{\varepsilon}\cdot\vartheta-T_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{\frac{2}{3}}(\vartheta)}}\bigr)\\
{}+\Sigma\Big(X^{\prime}q^{\frac{1}{3}}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-X_{\varepsilon}q^{\frac{1}{3}}(\vartheta)\Big)-\frac{2}{3}\Big(S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta)\Big)\,.\end{multlined}\end{array}\right.

Recall that
\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}
is provided by (
3.89
) with
(T,X,Y)
replaced by
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
; the same formula holds for
\vartheta
with
(T,X,Y)
replaced by
(T_{\varepsilon},X_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon})
. For such
\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}
(resp.
\vartheta
) we have
Y^{\prime}\cdot\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}=-|Y^{\prime}|+O(\gamma^{2})
(resp.
Y_{\varepsilon}\cdot\vartheta=-|Y_{\varepsilon}|+O(\gamma^{2})
). Setting
\gamma Z^{\prime}:={-|Y^{\prime}|+T^{\prime}}
and
\gamma Z_{\varepsilon}:={-|Y_{\varepsilon}|+T_{\varepsilon}}
,
\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
may be rewritten as

(3.104)

\quad\quad\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdots)=A\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdots)+\Sigma\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\cdots)\\
{}+Z^{\prime}-Z_{\varepsilon}-A(S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-S_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta))-\Big(\frac{S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}{3}-\frac{S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(A,\vartheta)}{3}\Big)+O(\gamma),

where all the small terms
O(\gamma)
come with differences
T^{\prime}q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-T_{\varepsilon}q^{2/3}(\vartheta)
,
q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)
,
X^{\prime}q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-X_{\varepsilon}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)
etc. Using (
3.1
) we bound the gradient of
\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}

(3.105)

(|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|)(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)\leq 4(|T^{\prime}q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-T_{\varepsilon}q^{2/3}(\vartheta)|\\
+|q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)|+|X^{\prime}q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-X_{\varepsilon}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)|+|Z^{\prime}-Z_{\varepsilon}|)\,.

As
N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(T,X,Y)
implies that
\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)\neq 0
for all
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}^{M}_{\gamma}(T,X,Y)
, it follows that the right hand side of (
3.105
) doesn’t vanish for any
(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)
. Hence, for all
(A,\Sigma,\rho)
on the support of the symbol and for every
\vartheta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-2}
,

(3.106)

(T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta),X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta),Z_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta))\notin B_{r_{0}}(T,X,Z),\quad\gamma Z={-|Y|+T}\,.

In fact, if the last statement does not hold, then there exist
(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)
such that

(|T-T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)|+|X-X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)|+|Z-Z_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)|\leq r_{0}.

Taking
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})=(T_{\varepsilon},X_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon})(A,\Sigma,\rho,\vartheta)
,
\gamma Z^{\prime}={-|Y^{\prime}|+T^{\prime}}
, then
\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}=\vartheta
and therefore the right hand side term in (
3.105
) vanishes which contradicts
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}^{M}(T,X,Y)
.
As (
3.106
) holds true for all
\vartheta
, it also holds for
\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}
from (
3.89
) (corresponding to
(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)
), and

|T-T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|+|X-X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|+|Z-Z_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|\geq r_{0}.

Moreover, at
(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)
and
\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}
we obtain from (
3.1
)

\displaystyle|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)=\frac{q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}|T-T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)=q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|X-X_{\varepsilon}|,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)\geq|Z-Z_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|-\frac{Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|T-T_{\varepsilon}|}{1+\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}-|\Sigma|q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|X^{\prime}-X_{\varepsilon}|,

and therefore there exists a uniform constant (depending only on
q
) such that

(3.107)

(|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|)(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)\geq C(|T-T_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|\\
+|X-X_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|+|Z^{\prime}-Z_{\varepsilon}(A,\Sigma,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})|)\geq Cr_{0}.

which allows to conclude the proof of Lemma
3.14
. ∎

Let now
a\sim\gamma
. We rescale variables as follows
s=\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S
,
\sigma\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma
,
\alpha=a|\theta|^{2/3}A
, let
\lambda=\lambda_{a}=\frac{a^{3/2}}{h}
and
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,a}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta):=\Phi^{M}_{N,a,a}(\sqrt{a}T,aX,\sqrt{a}Y,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S,a|\theta|^{2/3}A)
. The saddle points of
\Psi^{M}_{N,a,a}
satisfy
S^{2}=A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})
and they undergo coalescence when
A=q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})
. Let
\chi_{0}\in C^{\infty}
be a smooth cutoff, equal to
1
on
[0,\infty]
and equal to
0
on
[-\infty,-2]
. Then
(\chi_{0}Ai)(-(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}(A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})))
is a symbol of order
2/3
supported for values
(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}(A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}}))\leq 2
and
(1-\chi_{0})Ai(-(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}(A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})))
is supported for
A\geq q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})
and is equal to
1
on
(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}(A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}}))\geq 2
. Notice that on the support of
(1-\chi_{0})
the Airy function can be written as a sum of two contributions
A_{\pm}((|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}(A-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})))
corresponding to the saddle points
S_{\pm}
. We split the symbol of
V^{M}_{N}
in two parts using
\chi_{0}+(1-\chi_{0})=1
and notice that on the support of
\chi_{0}
the Airy function behaves as a symbol of order
2/3
.
Therefore we can write each integral
V^{M}_{N,a}(t,x,y)
as a sum
V^{M}_{N,a}(t,x,y)=\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{0,\pm\}}V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a}(t,x,y)
where for
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
,
V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N}
has phase
\tilde{\Psi}^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a,a}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,A,\rho)
while
V^{M,0}_{N,a}
has phase function
\Psi_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,0,A,\rho)
.
We are left to prove that for ever
\varepsilon\in\{0,\pm\}
the following holds

(3.108)

\sum_{N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1,M,\varepsilon}_{\Psi}(T,X,Y),|N|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{a}}V^{M,\varepsilon}_{N,a}(T,X,Y)=O(h^{\infty}).

For
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
we act exactly like in the transverse case
4a\leq\gamma
since on the support of
(1-\chi_{0})
we obtain two distinct saddle points
S_{\pm}
. For
\varepsilon=0
we use the fact that we got rid of variable
S
hence
\partial_{S}\Psi_{N,a,a}(T,X,Y,\Sigma,0,A,\rho)=0
and act again as in the previous case, completing the proof of Proposition
3.13
for the model Laplace
\Delta_{M}
.

We can now proceed with the proof of Proposition
3.13
in the general situation.
Recall that the phase of
V_{N}
is
\Phi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\sigma,s,\alpha,\theta)=t\tau_{q}(\alpha,\theta)+\Phi(x,y,\theta,\alpha,\sigma)-\Phi(a,0,\theta,\alpha,s)-NhL(\alpha^{3/2}/h)
(see (
3.15
)). Rescaling
s=\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}S
,
\alpha=\gamma|\theta|^{2/3}A

we obtain a new phase
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta):=\Phi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\sigma,s,\alpha,\theta)
whose saddle points
S_{\pm}
are solutions to the third equation in (
3.1
) that we re-write here, using (
2.65
),

(3.109)

S^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\Big(1+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 1}\Big)=A,

where terms in
\mathcal{H}_{j}
come with factors
O(\gamma^{j/2})
. As this equation is independent of
|\theta|
, we recover that
S_{\pm}=S_{\pm}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)
are independent of
|\theta|
or
N
.

Let first
4a\lesssim\gamma
; as
A
is close to
1
on the support of the symbol, critical points
S_{\pm}
are non-degenerate and have opposite signs.
Stationary phase in
S
yields
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}\in\{\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}\}
, where for
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
we set
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\theta):=\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{\varepsilon},A,\theta)
. Assume
t>0
. For
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, let

\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,y):=\{(N\in\mathbb{Z},\exists(\Sigma,A,\theta)\text{ such that }\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\theta)}\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\theta)=0\},

then
\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)=\cap_{\varepsilon=\pm}\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,y)
. Indeed, if
N\in\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)
, then there exists a critical point
\Sigma_{c},S_{c},A_{c},\theta_{c}
for the phase
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
;
S_{c}
satisfies (
3.109
), hence
S_{c}\in\{S_{\pm}\}
. Therefore
(\Sigma_{c},A_{c},\theta_{c})
is a critical point for
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{\pm}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A),A,\theta)=\tilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}
. Conversely, if
\Sigma_{\pm},A_{\pm},\theta_{\pm}
is a critical point for
\tilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{N,a,\gamma}
, then
(\Sigma_{\pm},S_{\pm}(a,\gamma,\vartheta_{{\pm}},A_{\pm}),A_{\pm},\theta_{\pm})
are critical points for
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
.
For
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
, let
\mathcal{N}^{1,\varepsilon}_{d}(t,x,y)=\cup_{\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)}\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
,
then we have
\mathcal{N}^{1}_{d}=\cap_{\pm}\mathcal{N}^{1,\pm}_{d}
and therefore,
(\mathcal{N}_{1})^{c}=\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}}(\mathcal{N}^{1,\varepsilon}_{d})^{c}
. Proposition
3.13
will follow from proving

(3.110)

\forall\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}\,,\quad\quad\sum_{N\notin\cup_{\pm}\mathcal{N}^{1,\pm}_{d}(t,x,y),|N|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}}V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)=O(h^{\infty})\,,

where
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)
has phase function
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
and symbol obtained from the one of
V_{N,\gamma}
after stationary phase in
S
.
For every
N
the phase
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
has two critical points with respect to
\vartheta
, which are non-degenerate. Indeed,
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
depends on
N
through
-NhL(|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})
and therefore its dependence on
\vartheta
comes only through
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{0,a,\gamma}=\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}|_{S=S_{\varepsilon}}
. Moreover,
\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}=|y|O(1)
,
(\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma})^{-1}=|y|^{-1}O(1)
and using Lemma
3.2
it follows that
|y|>c_{0}|t|
and in particular
y\neq 0
. Lemma
3.8
provide the explicit form of the critical point
{\vartheta_{c}}
and for
t>0
we set
{\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A)=\vartheta_{-}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{\varepsilon},A)
. Let
\rho:=|\theta|
and set

\displaystyle\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho):=\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{0,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho\vartheta_{c})-\frac{4}{3}N\rho\gamma^{3/2}A^{3/2}+NhB_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}(t,x,y):=\{(N\in\mathbb{Z},\exists(\Sigma,A,\rho)\text{ such that }\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)=0\}\,.

Remark again that
\mathcal{N}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}(t,x,y)=\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,y)
: indeed, if
N
is such that there exists a critical point
(\Sigma,A,\rho)
for
\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
at
(t,x,y)
, then
(\Sigma,A,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A))
is a critical point for
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
and the reverse statement holds as well. Let
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
and
N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1,\varepsilon}_{d}
. Then
N\notin\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
for all
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
and therefore for all such
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
we have

(3.111)

\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\theta)}\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\theta)\neq 0,\quad\forall(A,\Sigma,\rho).

This translates into
N\notin\mathcal{N}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
and
\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)\neq 0

for all
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
and all
\Sigma,A,\rho
on the support of the symbol of
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)
.

Lemma 3.16
.

There exists a uniform constant
c>0
such that, for all
N\notin\cup_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}}\mathcal{N}^{1,\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\Psi}}(t,x,y)
,

(3.112)

|\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\geq c\gamma^{3/2}

for all
(\Sigma,A,\rho)
on the support of the symbol of
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}
.

This lemma allows to perform non stationary phase in
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)
with
large parameter
\lambda_{\gamma}
and conclude that (
3.110
) holds true.

Remark 3.17
.

We need to define the integral curves of
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
: recall that (
3.1
) allows to parametrize the Lagrangian
\Lambda_{N}
; the projection
\pi_{N}(\Lambda_{N})
can be parametrized by
(\rho,\vartheta,\Sigma,A)
, with
\rho\sim 1
,
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
,
A,\Sigma,S
on the support of the symbol of
V_{N,\gamma}
and
S\in\{S_{\pm}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)\}
. We define the integral curves
\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}=\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma)
,
\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}=\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma)
,
\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}=\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma)
(as we did in Remark
3.14
in the model case) such that (
3.1
) holds at
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})
. Therefore, at a given point
(\rho,\vartheta,\Sigma,A)
,
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})
is defined such that (
3.1
) holds with
(t,x,y)=(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})
,
S=S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)
and
\theta=\rho\vartheta
.
Using Lemma
3.8
with
\vartheta\in\{\vartheta_{\pm}\}
and
(t,x,y)
replaced by
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})
, we obtain

(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\vartheta=-|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta)|+\gamma^{2}O(\frac{|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}|}{\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}})
.

Proof.

Let
(\Sigma,A,\rho)
be a point on the support of the symbol of
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)
, let
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
and define
S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)
to be the solution to the third equation in (
3.1
) (where
\vartheta
is replaced by
\vartheta
) with sign
\varepsilon
. Let
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}):=(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma)
denote the corresponding point on the integral curve (as in Remark
3.17
); then, by construction of the integral curves,

\nabla_{(A,\Sigma,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\Sigma,A,\rho)=\nabla_{(A,\Sigma,\rho)}\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}((\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,\cdots,\gamma),\Sigma,A,\rho\vartheta)=0\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,.

Using the explicit form of (
3.1
), this translates into

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma q^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))+\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Sigma-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A))=2N\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}A^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{\frac{3}{2}}))+O(\gamma^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta)+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))=\gamma(A-\Sigma^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}\Big((\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\vartheta+\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}\Big)-(1-\tau_{q}(A,\vartheta))(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\\
+\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big[\frac{\Sigma^{3}}{3}+\Sigma\Big(\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta)+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-A\Big)-\frac{S_{\varepsilon}^{3}}{3}-S_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-A\Big)\Big]\\
=\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}NA^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{\frac{3}{2}}))+O(\gamma^{2})\,,\end{multlined}

where the last equation is obtained using the second to last equation in the system (
3.1
), as satisfied by
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma),\Sigma,S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A),A,\rho,\vartheta
. Let
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
and let
\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}:={\vartheta_{c}}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A)
be the critical point of
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho\vartheta)
, then, for any
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\begin{multlined}\partial_{A}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}=\frac{\gamma q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}}(t^{\prime}-B_{2}(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}))\\
{}-\frac{\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))-\gamma^{3/2}(S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c},A)-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A))+O(\gamma^{2})\,,\end{multlined}

(3.115)

\displaystyle\partial_{\Sigma}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}=\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\Bigl(q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})\frac{x^{\prime}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})+O({\gamma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr)-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}\bigr(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta)+O({\gamma}^{\frac{1}{2}}))\bigl)\,,

(3.116)

\partial_{\rho}\Psi^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}=((y^{\prime}+\nabla B_{0}(y^{\prime},\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}))\cdot\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}+t^{\prime})-((\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\cdot\vartheta+\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon})\\
{}+\frac{\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})}}(t^{\prime}-B_{2}(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}))-\frac{\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}{2\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)}}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\\
{}+\gamma^{3/2}\Sigma\Big(\frac{x^{\prime}}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})(1+\ell(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta)+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))\Big)\\
{}-\frac{2}{3}\gamma^{3/2}\Big(S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c},A)-S^{3}_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)\Big)+O(\gamma^{2})\,,

where in the last line we used
\frac{S_{\varepsilon}^{3}}{3}+S_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta)(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-A\Big)=-\frac{2}{3}S^{3/2}_{\varepsilon}+O(\sqrt{\gamma})
.

Remark 3.18
.

All terms
O(\gamma^{2})
are smooth functions of differences:
\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}-\vartheta
,
q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})(t^{\prime}-B_{2}(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}))-q^{2/3}(\vartheta)(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))
,
q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})\frac{x^{\prime}}{\gamma}-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}
as well as
(f(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-f(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))
where
f
is either
B_{2}(y,\vartheta)
or
\nabla B_{2}(y,\vartheta)
and such that
f(y,\vartheta)=O(|y|^{2})
or
f\in\{B_{2k}(y,\vartheta),\nabla B_{2k}(y,\vartheta)\}
is such that
f(y,\vartheta)=O(|y|)
and coefficients
O(\gamma^{2+k})
instead of
O(\gamma^{2})
, or
f\in\{\alpha_{j}(y,\vartheta),\gamma_{j}(y,\vartheta)\}
(where
\alpha_{j},\gamma_{j}
are coefficients of homogeneous terms of degree
2j
in
A_{\Gamma}
) with coefficients
O(\gamma^{2+j/2})
.

We then obtain and upper bound for
\nabla_{A,\Sigma,\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}
(similar to (
3.105
))

(3.117)

\gamma^{-3/2}(|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|)(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)\\
\leq C\Big(\Big|q^{2/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})\frac{(t^{\prime}-B_{2}(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-q^{2/3}(\vartheta)\frac{(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big|\\
+|q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)|(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))+|q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})\frac{x^{\prime}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}_{c}))-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))|\\
+\Big|\frac{(y^{\prime}+\nabla B_{0}(y^{\prime},\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}))\cdot\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}+t^{\prime}}{\gamma^{3/2}}-\frac{(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\vartheta))\cdot\vartheta+\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|+O(\sqrt{\gamma})|y^{\prime}-\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}|\Big),

where the term
|q^{1/3}(\vartheta^{\prime}_{c})-q^{1/3}(\vartheta)|
comes from
\Big(S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c},A)-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)\Big)
and the term
O(\sqrt{\gamma})|y^{\prime}-\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}|
comes from differences involving
B_{2k}
,
\alpha_{j}
,
\gamma_{j}
. The constant
C>0
depends on
q
.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
(t,x,y)
be fixed, let
(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta)
belong to the support of the symbol of
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}
, then
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta,a,\gamma)\notin\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
.

Proof.

If not, then taking
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime}):=(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,\vartheta)
we have
\vartheta_{c}^{\prime}=\vartheta
,
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}
and
\nabla_{A,\Sigma,\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime},\Sigma,A,\rho)=0
, which cannot happen for
N\notin\mathcal{N}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
.
∎

We now prove (
3.112
): from the previous lemma, for all
A,\Sigma,\rho
we have
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}},a,\gamma)\notin\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t,x,y)
, where
{\vartheta_{c}}={\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A)
is the critical point of
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho\vartheta)
given in Lemma
3.8
(with
{\vartheta_{c}}=\vartheta_{-}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A)
for
t>0
).
This yields

(3.118)

\Big|\frac{t-B_{2}(y,\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-\frac{\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big|+|\frac{x}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y,\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)))-\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})))|\\
+\Big|\frac{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y))|-t}{\gamma^{3/2}}-\frac{|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}))|-\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|>r_{0}.

As (
3.1
),(
3.115
) and (
3.116
) do hold for any
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
, taking
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=(t,x,y)
and
\vartheta:={\vartheta_{c}}(=\vartheta_{c}^{\prime})={\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A)
yields
S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta^{\prime}_{c},A)=S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,\vartheta,A)
and therefore, for all
(A,\Sigma,\rho)
on the support of
V^{\varepsilon}_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)
and
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})=(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})
we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})\Big|\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-\frac{(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big|+O(\sqrt{\gamma})\,,

\displaystyle\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)

\displaystyle=q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|\frac{x}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))|+O(\sqrt{\gamma})\,,

\displaystyle\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{multlined}\Big|\frac{(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+t)}{\gamma^{3/2}}-\frac{(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon})}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|\\
-\frac{1}{2}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})\Big|\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-\frac{(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big|\\
-q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|\frac{x}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))|-O(\sqrt{\gamma}),\end{multlined}

and therefore there exists a uniform constant
C>0
(depending only on
q
) such that

(3.119)

C\gamma^{-3/2}(|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|+|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|)(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)\\
\geq\Big[\Big|\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}-\frac{(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}-B_{2}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\Big|+|\frac{x}{\gamma}(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\frac{\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma}(1+\ell(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))|\\
+\Big|\frac{(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+t)}{\gamma^{3/2}}-\frac{(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon})}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|-O(\sqrt{\gamma})\Big].

As
(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}=-|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})|+O(\gamma^{2})
by Lemma
3.8
,
(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}=-|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}})|+O(\gamma^{2})
by construction and Lemma
3.8
, the last line in (
3.119
) may be rewritten as

\Big|\frac{|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})|-t}{\gamma^{3/2}}-\frac{|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}})|-\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma^{3/2}}\Big|-O(\sqrt{\gamma}).

As
(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})=(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{x}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})(\Sigma,A,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}})
is the integral curve associated to
(\Sigma,A,\rho,{\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A))
, it follows from the definition of
\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)
in (
3.16
) and Lemma
3.12
(applied to
{\vartheta_{c}}
and
\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})
) that

(3.120)

|\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon})-{\vartheta_{c}}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A)|=O(\gamma^{3/2}/\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon})\,,

which further yields

|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},{\vartheta_{c}})|-|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}+\nabla B_{0}(\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{\vartheta}(\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon},\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}))|=O(\frac{|\tilde{y}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\tilde{t}_{\varepsilon}}\gamma^{3/2})\,.

We now consider the difference between
{\vartheta_{c}}
and
\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)
: the assumption of Proposition
3.13
is
\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)\neq\emptyset
, hence there exists
N_{0}\in\mathcal{N}(t,x,y)
for which
\tilde{\Phi}_{N_{0},a,\gamma}
is stationary with respect to
A,\rho,\vartheta
and from Lemma
3.10
it follows that (
3.67
) must hold. Then
\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)
is an approximation modulo
O(\gamma^{3/2}/t)
of the critical point
\vartheta
(when we consider all variables
(A,\rho,\vartheta)
) satisfying (
3.68
). From the formulas (
3.58
) for
{\vartheta_{c}}
and (
3.68
) for
\vartheta_{c}^{\sharp}
, we always have
|{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|\lesssim\gamma
, but this is not small enough to conclude. If
|{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|\lesssim\gamma^{3/2}/t
, then, using (
3.118
), (
3.119
), (
3.120
), we obtain

|(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}-(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)))\cdot\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)|=O(|y|^{2}\gamma^{3/2}/t)=O(|y|\gamma^{3/2}).

Suppose that, with
{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}
given in (
3.68
) from Lemma
3.10
, we have

\gamma\gtrsim|{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|\gtrsim\gamma^{3/2}/t
such that

(3.121)

|(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}-(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|\geq r_{0}\gamma^{3/2}\,.

Let
C_{q}\geq 2
be such that
\|q(\vartheta)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{q}
. If the first line in (
3.118
) is bounded from below by
r_{0}/(10C_{q})
, then using
|{\vartheta_{c}}-\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)|\leq|{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|+|{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}-\tilde{\vartheta}(t,y)|\lesssim\gamma
, the second line in (
3.119
) is bounded from below by
r_{0}/(20C_{q})
(if
\sqrt{\gamma}
is small compared to
r_{0}
) and we are done. Therefore we are reduced to considering the first line in (
3.118
) bounded from above by
r_{0}/(10C_{q})
, which is the same as assuming that
|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\leq\gamma^{3/2}{r_{0}}/{10}
and
|\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\leq\gamma^{3/2}{r_{0}}/{10}
.
We prove that, if, moreover, (
3.121
) holds, then
\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\geq r_{0}/5
. As
\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{A}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,y,\Sigma,A,\rho)|\leq\frac{r_{0}}{10}
,
we find

(3.122)

\Big|\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{2}\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+(\Sigma-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}},A))+O(\sqrt{\gamma})-2NA^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B^{\prime}_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}))\Big|\leq r_{0}/10\,.

Recall that

(3.123)

\gamma^{-3/2}\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}=\gamma^{-3/2}\Big((y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+t\Big)-\frac{Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{2}\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\\
