+\Big(\Sigma^{3}/3+\Sigma(\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))+\frac{2}{3}S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}},A)\Big)-\frac{4}{3}NA^{3/2}(1-B^{\prime}_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}))+O(\sqrt{\gamma})

and therefore, by substitution in the
NA^{3/2}
term,

(3.124)

\left|\gamma^{-3/2}\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}-\left(\gamma^{-3/2}\Big((y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}+t\Big)-\frac{Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{6}\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\right.\right.\\
{}+\left.\left.\Big(\Sigma^{3}/3+\Sigma\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))+\frac{2}{3}S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}},A)-\frac{2}{3}(\Sigma-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}},A))\Big)\right)\right|\leq r_{0}/10\,.

With
{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}
given by (
3.68
) in Lemma
3.10
(recall that it is independent of
N
), we have

(3.125)

\gamma^{-3/2}\Big((y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}+t\Big)-\frac{Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp})}{6}\frac{(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}\\
+\Big(\Sigma^{3}/3+\Sigma\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))+\frac{2}{3}S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp},A)-\frac{2}{3}(\Sigma-S_{\varepsilon}(a,\gamma,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp},A))\Big)+O(\sqrt{\gamma})=0.

Taking the difference between the last two equations and using that
|{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}|\lesssim\gamma
and that differences always provide functions of
{\vartheta_{c}}-{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}
, we get, using (
3.121
)

\gamma^{-3/2}|\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}|=\gamma^{-3/2}\Big|(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}-(y+\nabla B_{0}(y,{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}))\cdot{\vartheta_{c}}^{\sharp}\Big|-O(\sqrt{\gamma})-r_{0}/10\geq\frac{r_{0}}{5}\,.

Therefore we always have
\Big|\nabla_{(\Sigma,A,\rho)}\tilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}_{N,a,\gamma}\Big|\gtrsim r_{0}/5
, which completes the proof in the case
4a\lesssim\gamma
.∎

Let now
\gamma\sim a
. We write
\Phi_{N,a,a}:=\Phi_{N,a,\gamma\sim a}
and rescale variables as follows
s=\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S
,
\sigma\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma
,
\alpha=a|\theta|^{2/3}A
and let
\lambda=\lambda_{a}=\frac{a^{3/2}}{h}
. We define

(3.126)

\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta):=\Phi_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S,a|\theta|^{2/3}A,\theta).

Let
s_{0}(a,\theta,\alpha)
be the unique solution to
\partial^{2}_{s}\Phi(a,0,\theta,\alpha,s)=0
and let
s_{\pm}(a,\theta,\alpha)
be the critical points of
\Phi(a,0,\theta,\alpha)
(see Lemma
6.9
from the Appendix for details on critical points
s_{\pm}
of
\Phi(x,y,\theta,\alpha,\sigma)
), then after rescaling variables we obtain at most two critical points such that

S_{\pm}(a,\vartheta,A)-S_{0}(a,\vartheta,A)=\pm\sqrt{\frac{\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)}{a}}(1+O(\sqrt{\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)})),

where
\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)/a=A-e_{0}(a,0,\vartheta,aA)
is the phase function introduced in Theorem
2.1
, with
e_{0}
elliptic and close to
1
. In this case
\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)/a
is close to
0
and
S_{\pm}
are real only for
\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)\geq 0
. We now repeat the argument from the model case : let
\chi_{0}\in C^{\infty}
be a smooth cutoff, equal to
1
on
[0,\infty]
and equal to
0
on
[-\infty,-2]
. Then
(\chi_{0}Ai)(-(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA))
is a symbol of order
2/3
supported for values
(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)\leq 2
and
(1-\chi_{0})Ai(-(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}\zeta(\cdots)))
is supported on
A\geq e_{0}(a,0,\vartheta,aA)
with value
1
on
(|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)\geq 2
. On the support of
(1-\chi_{0})
the Airy function may be written as a sum of two contributions
A_{\pm}((|\theta|\lambda)^{2/3}\zeta(a,0,\vartheta,aA)))
corresponding to critical points
S_{\pm}
. We split the symbol of
V_{N}
in two parts using
\chi_{0}+(1-\chi_{0})=1
and on the support of
\chi_{0}
the Airy function behaves as a symbol of order
2/3
. Therefore we write each integral
V_{N,a}(t,x,y)
as a sum
V_{N,a}(t,x,y)=\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{0,\pm\}}V^{\varepsilon}_{N,a}(t,x,y)
where for
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
,
V^{\varepsilon}_{N}
has phase
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{\varepsilon},A,\theta)
while
V^{0}_{N,a}
has phase
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{0}(a,\vartheta,A),A,\theta)
. It remains to prove that, for each
\varepsilon\in\{0,\pm\}
,

(3.127)

\sum_{N\notin\mathcal{N}^{1}_{d}(t,x,y),|N|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{a}}V^{\varepsilon}_{N,a}(t,x,y)=O(h^{\infty}).

For
\varepsilon\in\{\pm\}
we act exactly like in the transverse case
4a\leq\gamma
, as on the support of
(1-\chi_{0})
we have two separate critical points
S_{\pm}
. For
\varepsilon=0
we use that we got rid of variable
S
hence
\partial_{S}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{0}(a,\vartheta,A),A,\theta)
and act again as in the previous case, finally completing the proof of Proposition
3.13
.
∎

In the last part of this section we prove Theorem
3.1
: writing
\mathcal{P}_{h,a}
as the sum over
\gamma
,
we evaluate each
\mathcal{P}_{h,a,\gamma}
and then sum up in
a\lesssim\gamma\ll 1
. We deal separately with the cases
\gamma>4a
and
\gamma\sim a
when use the notation
\mathcal{P}_{h,a,a}
for
\mathcal{P}_{h,a,\gamma\sim a}
and
V_{N,a}
for
V_{N,\gamma\sim a}
. From (
2.54
) and (
3.9
) we have

V_{N}=\sum_{a\lesssim\gamma\ll 1}V_{N,\gamma}=V_{N,a}+\sum_{4a\leq\gamma}V_{N,\gamma}\,.

Remark 3.20
.

In (
3.9
) we set
\omega=h^{-2/3}\alpha
, and then
\alpha=\gamma A
, which yields
\omega=\lambda_{\gamma}^{2/3}A
, with
\lambda_{\gamma}=\gamma^{3/2}/h
and
A\sim 1
on the support of
\psi
. Since the ”main” contribution in the parametrix (
2.49
) comes from values
\omega\sim\lambda^{2/3}
with
\lambda=\lambda_{a}=\frac{a^{3/2}}{h}
(or, in terms of
\mathcal{P}_{h,a}
written as a sum (
2.50
) with
\omega_{k}\sim k^{2/3}
, the main contribution comes from
k\sim\lambda
), the part
\mathcal{P}_{h,a,a}
will provide the ”worst” case scenario.

Before stating estimates for
V_{N,\gamma}
, when
|N|\geq 1
and
a\lesssim\gamma
, we recall that we have the free space dispersion for
V_{0}
, assuming that
t>h
so that the dispersive effect takes over:

(3.128)

|V_{0}(t,x,y)|\leq Ch^{-d}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\,.

Proposition 3.21
.

Assume
\epsilon>0
,
a\in[h^{2/3-\epsilon},a_{0}]
, and
h\in(0,1)
. Then there exists
C(\epsilon,a_{0})
such that, for
t\gtrsim h
,

(3.129)

|V_{\pm 1}(t,x,y)|\leq Ch^{-d}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{1/2}+a^{1/4}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{1/4}+h^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\,.

Proposition 3.22
.

Assume
\epsilon>0
,
a\in[h^{2/3-\epsilon},a_{0}]
,
\gamma\geq 4a
,
h\in(0,1)
and let
\lambda_{\gamma}=\gamma^{3/2}/h
. Then there exists
C(\epsilon,a_{0})
such that, for
t\gtrsim\sqrt{\gamma}
,

(3.130)

|\sum_{|N|\geq 2}V_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)|\leq Ch^{-d}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}h^{1/3}\left(\frac{\gamma^{1/4}}{t^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{\gamma}^{3/2}}\right)\,.

Proposition 3.23
.

Assume
\epsilon>0
,
a\in[h^{2/3-\epsilon},a_{0}]
,
h\in(0,1)
and let
\lambda=a^{3/2}/h
. Then there exists
C(\epsilon,a_{0})
such that for
0\leq x\leq 2a
,

(3.131)

|\sum_{|N|\geq 2}V_{N,a}(t,x,y)|\leq Ch^{-d}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(a^{1/4}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{1/4}+\frac{h^{1/3}}{\lambda^{4/3}}\right)\,.

Our main estimate, (
3.2
) from Theorem
3.1
, follows at once from the previous propositions, in the regime
a\geq h^{2/3-\epsilon}
. Recall that for
a\lesssim\gamma
,
V_{N,\gamma}
was defined in (
3.9
) as

(3.132)

V_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi h^{d+1}}\int e^{\frac{i}{h}(t\tau_{q}(\alpha,\theta)+\Phi(x,y,\theta,\alpha,\sigma)-\Phi(a,0,\theta,\alpha,s)-NhL(h^{-2/3}\alpha))}\psi(\alpha/(|\theta|^{2/3}\gamma))\\
{}\times{\chi^{\flat}}(\alpha/\epsilon_{0}){\chi^{\sharp}}(\alpha/h^{2/3})\chi(s)p_{h}(x,y,\theta,\alpha,\sigma)\tilde{q}_{h}(\theta,\alpha,s)ds\,d\theta d{\sigma}d\alpha\,,

where the symbol of
V_{N,\gamma}
(the same for every
N
) is of order
0
. Let
|N|\geq 1
and
a\lesssim\gamma
, and set
\lambda_{\gamma}=\frac{\gamma^{3/2}}{h}
. We rescale variables as follows

•

If
\gamma\sim a
we set
s=\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S
,
\sigma\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma
,
\alpha=a|\theta|^{2/3}A
and let
\lambda=\frac{a^{3/2}}{h}
;

•

If
4a\leq\gamma
we set

s=\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}S
,
\sigma\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma
,
\alpha=\gamma|\theta|^{2/3}A
.

We let
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)
be given by (
3.126
) when
\gamma\sim a
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)
be given by (
3.48
) when
4a\leq\gamma
. We have (including the case where
\gamma
is replaced by
a
)

\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)=\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)-\frac{4}{3}N\gamma^{3/2}|\theta|A^{3/2}+NhB_{L}(|\theta|\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}).

The phase
\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)
, with
\theta=\rho\vartheta
,
|\vartheta|=1
, has two critical points
\vartheta_{\pm}
and they are non-degenerate. Indeed (see the proof of Proposition
3.4
),
\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}=|y|O(1)
for any
a\lesssim\gamma
. From Lemma
3.2
we have
|y|\geq c_{0}|t|
and stationary phase in
\vartheta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
yields a decay factor

(3.133)

\left(\frac{h}{|y|}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\leq C\left(\frac{h}{|t|}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}},\text{ for }|y|\geq c_{0}|t|.

Recall that the critical points (in
\vartheta
) of
\Phi_{N,a,\gamma}
are (
3.58
) (Lemma
3.8
). According to Remark
3.9
, if we fix a sign for
t
, only one of these critical points provide non-trivial contributions. Let
t>0
and denote
\vartheta_{c}=\vartheta_{-}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A)
the critical point in
\vartheta
,and let

\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)=\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho\vartheta_{c})\,.

Recall from Lemma
3.8
that
{\vartheta_{c}}
doesn’t depend on
N
, neither on
\rho=|\theta|
(since
\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\theta)
is linear in
|\theta|
).

Lemma 3.24
.

The critical points
{\vartheta_{c}}
are such that

(3.134)

\partial_{\Sigma}{\vartheta_{c}}=O(\gamma^{3/2}/t),\quad\partial_{S}{\vartheta_{c}}=O(\gamma^{3/2}/t).

Proof.

We evaluate its derivatives in
\Sigma
and
S
of
{\vartheta_{c}}
using the equation (
3.58
) from Lemma
3.8
.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

For
|N|\leq\lambda_{\gamma}
, the factor
e^{iNB_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})}
does not oscillate. Indeed
NB_{L}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2})\sim N/\lambda_{\gamma}\lesssim 1
, and moving this factor to the symbol, the phase becomes linear in
\rho
. On the other hand, as soon as
N>\lambda_{\gamma}
we can take advantage of the stationary phase in
\rho
, as we shall see below. Applying the stationary phase in
\rho
turns out to be of particular interest for
N\geq\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
, since in this case for a given
t
such that
t/\sqrt{\gamma}\geq\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
, the cardinal of the set
\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
is not uniformly bounded anymore but starts to increase like
\frac{t}{\sqrt{\gamma}\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}}
.

3.2.
The tangent part
\gamma\sim a
.

Let first
a\sim\gamma
and set
\lambda=\lambda_{a}=a^{3/2}/h
. We apply stationary phase to
\Psi_{N,a,a}
with respect to
A
.

Lemma 3.26
.

The equation
\partial_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}=0
has at most one solution on the support of the symbol
\psi(A)
, that we denote
A_{c}
. Then
A_{c}
is a non-degenerate critical point and
\partial^{2}_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}|_{A=A_{c}}\sim Na^{3/2}
.

Proof.

The phase is stationary when
\partial_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}=\partial_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}=0
. The equation
\partial_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}=0
is the first in (
3.1
) and we easily see that for any
N\neq 0
there exists a solution
A^{1/2}_{c}\sim\frac{t}{4N\sqrt{a}}
. For
N\sim t/(4\sqrt{a})
we have
A_{c}\sim 1
. Moreover,

\partial^{2}_{A}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}=\partial^{2}_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}+\partial_{A}{\vartheta_{c}}\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}\,.

The derivative with respect to
A
of the first term in the left hand side of (
3.1
) is
t\,O(a^{2})
. Using (
3.56
),
\partial^{2}_{A}(\Phi(x,y,\theta,a|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma)-\Phi(a,0,\theta,a|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S))=|y|O(a^{2})
. As the derivative of the coefficient of
N
in (
3.1
) is close to
Na^{3/2}
and
|N|\sim|t|/4\sqrt{a}
,
|t|\sim|y|
, the main contribution of
\partial^{2}_{A}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}
is also
Na^{3/2}
. On the other hand,

(3.135)

\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}=\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}\sim tO(a).

Moreover, taking the derivative with respect to
A
of
\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}=0
gives
\partial_{A}{\vartheta_{c}}\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}=-\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}
; using
\Big(\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}\Big)^{-1}=|y|^{-1}O(1)
,
|y|\geq c_{0}|t|
and (
3.135
) eventually yields

\partial_{A}{\vartheta_{c}}\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}\sim\Big|\partial_{A}\nabla_{\vartheta}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}\Big|^{2}\times|y|^{-1}O(1)\sim\frac{t^{2}}{|y|}O(a^{2})\lesssim\frac{|t|}{c_{0}}O(a^{2})\,.

Therefore the main contribution of
\partial^{2}_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}
is
Na^{3/2}
.
∎

Hence we may apply stationary phase in
A
and get another decay factor

(3.136)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{a^{3/2}|N|}}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{1/h}}=\frac{1}{(\lambda|N|)^{1/2}}\,.

Lemma 3.27
.

Assume
N\sqrt{a}\lesssim 1
. The critical point
A_{c}
is

(3.137)

\displaystyle A_{c}=\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}-2\mathbb{A}_{0}\mathbb{A}_{1}+\Big(\frac{\Sigma}{2N}-\frac{S}{2N}\Big)^{2}+O\Big(a(\frac{\Sigma}{N},\frac{S}{N})^{2}\Big)+\frac{f_{0}}{N\lambda^{2}}\,,

(3.138)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{0}=\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})|_{\Sigma=S=0}}{4N\sqrt{a}}(t+E_{0}(y,a)),\quad\mathbb{A}_{1}=\frac{\Sigma}{2N}(1-xE_{1})-\frac{S}{2N}(1-aE_{2})\,,

and
f_{0}
is an asymptotic expansion in
\lambda^{-1}
,
E_{0}=O(|y|^{2},a|y|)
,
E_{1,2}=O(1)
,
E_{0,1,2}
are independent of
\Sigma
and
S
.

Proof.

At the critical point
A_{c}
, (
3.1
) holds (replacing
\vartheta
with
{\vartheta_{c}}
and
\gamma
with
a
). Using (
3.56
),

(3.139)

A^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda A^{3/2}))=\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\Big(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\Big)}{4N\sqrt{a}\sqrt{1+aAq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})}\\
+\frac{(-\Sigma+S)}{2N}+\frac{{a}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{2N\sqrt{1+aAq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big[\frac{x}{a^{3/2}}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})}\\
-\frac{a}{a^{3/2}}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})}\Big]\,,

where we recall that
B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})=O(|y|)
for all
k\geq 2
and
1-\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})=\frac{aAq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{(1+\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}}))}
.

Remark 3.28
.

When
|N|\lesssim\lambda
, according to Remark
3.25
we get rid of the factor
(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda A^{3/2}))
in the LHS of (
3.139
), without which the equation satisfied by
A_{c}
is independent of
\rho,\lambda
.

At
\Sigma=S=0
, we get for
A_{c}^{1/2}|_{\Sigma=S=0}

(3.140)

A^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda A^{\frac{3}{2}}))=\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/(4N\sqrt{a})}{\sqrt{1+aAq^{\frac{2}{3}}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big[t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\\
+2a\Big(\frac{x}{a}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(x,y,0,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)-\frac{a}{a}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(a,0,0,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)\Big)\Big]_{|\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}}),\Sigma=S=0}\,.

Using that
\partial_{\tau}(\tau A_{\Gamma})=O(1)
and the expansion (
2.28
) of
B_{L}
, by the implicit function theorem applied to (
3.140
), we obtain that
A^{1/2}_{c}|_{\Sigma=S=0}
is of the form
\mathbb{A}_{0}
given in (
3.138
).
Taking the derivative of (
3.139
) with respect to
\Sigma
yields

(3.141)

\partial_{\Sigma}(A_{c}^{1/2})\Big(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(z)-\frac{9}{4}zB_{L}^{\prime\prime}(z)\Big)|_{z=\rho\lambda A^{3/2}}=\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{4N\sqrt{a}\sqrt{1+aA_{c}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big[\partial_{\Sigma}{\vartheta_{c}}\cdot\Big(-\nabla B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\\
+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)\nabla B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\Big)+(\partial_{\Sigma}A_{c}\partial_{A}\tau+\partial_{\Sigma}{\vartheta_{c}}\cdot\nabla_{\vartheta}\tau)\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial^{2}_{\tau,\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\Big]\Big|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA_{c},{\vartheta_{c}})}\\
+\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{\sqrt{1+aA_{c}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big)\frac{\Big(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\Big)}{4N\sqrt{a}}\Big|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA_{c},{\vartheta_{c}})}\\
-\frac{1}{2N}+\frac{\sqrt{a}}{2N}\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{\sqrt{1+aA_{c}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big)\frac{x}{a}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})}\\
+\frac{\sqrt{a}}{2N}\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{\sqrt{1+aA_{c}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\frac{x}{a}\partial_{\Sigma}\Big[\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)|_{\tau=\tau_{q}(aA,{\vartheta_{c}})}\Big]\,,

where
\partial_{A}\tau_{q}=aq^{2/3}(\vartheta)/(2\tau_{q})
,
B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})=O(|y|)
,
\nabla_{\vartheta}\tau_{q}=aA\nabla(q^{2/3}(\vartheta))/(2\tau_{q})
and
\partial_{\Sigma}(\partial_{\tau}(\tau A_{\Gamma}))=O(a)
. At
\Sigma=S=0
we find

\partial_{\Sigma}(A_{c}^{1/2})|_{\Sigma=S=0}=\frac{2}{3}\partial_{\Sigma}{\vartheta_{c}}\nabla q({\vartheta_{c}})q^{-1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\frac{(t+O(|y|^{2})+O(a|y|))}{4N\sqrt{a}}-\frac{1}{2N}(1+O(a^{3/2})+O(\lambda^{-2}))\,,

which, together with (
3.139
) and (
3.134
), allows to obtain the explicit form of the derivative of
A_{c}
with respect to
\Sigma
(similar computations hold for
S
).
∎

Remark 3.29
.

When
|N|\geq\lambda^{2}
, stationary phase in
\rho
turns out to be of particular interest: for a given
t
such that
t/\sqrt{a}\geq\lambda^{2}
, the cardinal of the set
\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
is large. Obtaining a bound of the integral defining
V_{N,a}
better than the one given by integrating only with respect to
\Sigma,S
turns out to be crucial in order to obtain the desired dispersive estimates.

Let us consider the case
|N|>C\lambda^{2}
for some constant
C>1
. Let

(3.142)

\phi_{N,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,\rho):=\Psi_{N,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A_{c},\rho)

denote the critical value of
\Psi_{N,a,a}
. We compute derivatives of
\phi_{N,a}
with respect to
\rho
.
Since
\vartheta_{c}
is independent of
\rho
,
the phase
\phi_{N,a}
is stationary in
\rho
when

0=\partial_{\rho}\phi_{N,a}=(\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,a}+\partial_{\rho}A_{c}\partial_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a})|_{A_{c}}=\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,a}|_{A_{c}}\,,

with
A_{c}
provided in (
3.137
), where the coefficients of
t,\Sigma,S
(in the first three terms of the sum) are independent of
\rho
(notice that in the equation (
3.139
) verified by
A_{c}
, the only term containing
\rho
is
B_{L}^{\prime}
which amounts for the terms with factor
O(\lambda^{-2})
in (
3.137
)). Since
\Psi_{N,a,a}=\Psi_{0,a,a}-\frac{4}{3}a^{3/2}\rho NA^{3/2}+NhB_{L}(\rho\lambda A^{3/2})
, and since
\Psi_{0,a,a}-\frac{4}{3}a^{3/2}\rho NA^{3/2}
is linear in
\rho
, the second derivative is

\partial^{2}_{\rho}\phi_{N,a}=\Big(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,a}+\partial_{\rho}A_{c}(\partial_{\rho}\partial_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}+\partial_{\rho}A_{c}\partial^{2}_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a})\Big)|_{A_{c}}\,.

For
|N|>\lambda^{2}\gg\lambda
, the only part that matters here is the contribution from
\frac{N}{\lambda}B_{L}(\rho\lambda A^{3/2})
. Taking into account that the support of the symbol of
V_{N,a}
in
A
is a fixed, compact set of
(0,\infty)
, the contribution of
\partial^{2}_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,a}
will be
hN/\lambda
.
The main contribution of
\partial_{\rho}\partial_{A}\Psi_{N,a,a}|_{A_{c}}
is also of size
hN/\lambda
, but it comes with a factor
\partial_{\rho}A_{c}
, which, from (
3.137
), can be at most
O(1/N)
, since the first three terms in the sum defining
A_{c}
do not depend on
\rho
. Eventually we obtain,

(3.143)

\frac{1}{h}|\partial^{2}_{\rho}\phi_{N,a}|\sim\frac{|N|}{\lambda}\,,

hence the stationary phase in
\rho
will produce a factor
(\frac{|N|}{\lambda})^{-1/2}

which is (of interest only in the regime
|N|\geq C\lambda
and) particularly useful for
\frac{|N|}{\lambda}>\lambda
.
Let
\sigma_{V,h,a}(\Sigma,S,\rho)
be obtained from

\psi(A)p_{h}(x,y,\theta,a|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma)\tilde{q}_{h}(\theta,a|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}S)

after applying the stationary phase in both
\vartheta
and
A
. We note that
\sigma_{V,h,a}
is independent of
N
, of order zero and has compact support in
\Sigma,S
. We are left with estimating the oscillatory integral
\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}d\Sigma dSd\rho
.
Following the approach in
[
12
]
(see also
[
9
]
), we deal separately with
|N|>\lambda^{1/3}
and
|N|\leq\lambda^{1/3}
, where we recall that
\lambda=\lambda_{a}=a^{3/2}/h
.

3.2.1.
Large number of reflections:
|N|>\lambda^{1/3}

Lemma 3.30
.

There exists
C
(independent of
N
) such that, if
|N|\geq\lambda^{1/3}
,

(3.144)

\left|\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\Sigma,S,\rho)d\Sigma dSd\rho\right|\leq\frac{C}{\lambda^{2/3}}\,.

Remark 3.31
.

The proof will be split in two parts: first, we consider
\lambda^{1/3}\leq|N|\leq\lambda^{2}
where we integrate with respect to
S,\Sigma
and then bound the remaining integral in
\rho
owning to its compact support; then for
|N|>\lambda^{2}
we start with stationary phase in
\rho
, which provides additional decay; then we integrate with respect to
S,\Sigma
following closely the approach for
\lambda^{1/3}\leq|N|\leq\lambda^{2}
.

Proof.

We compute derivatives of
\phi_{N,a}
with respect to
\Sigma,S
. Start with
\lambda^{1/3}\leq|N|\leq\lambda^{2}
and follow
[
12
, Proposition 4]
(see also
[
9
, Lemma 2.24]
, where only the case
N<\lambda^{2}
is considered).
Using
\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}
as provided in (
3.49
) for
N=0
and
\gamma
replaced by
a
, we compute

(3.145)

\partial_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a}=\partial_{\Sigma}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}|_{{\vartheta_{c}},A_{c},\rho=|\theta|}=a^{3/2}\rho\Big[\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A_{c}\\
+\frac{x}{a}\frac{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}{\sqrt{a}}\partial_{\Sigma}A_{\Gamma}\Big(x,y,\frac{\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})},\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}\Big)\Big]\\
=a^{3/2}\rho\Big[\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A_{c}+\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big].

In the same way we have

(3.146)

\partial_{S}\phi_{N,a}=\partial_{S}\tilde{\Phi}_{0,a,a}|_{{\vartheta_{c}},A_{c},\rho=|\theta|}=-a^{3/2}\rho\Big[S^{2}+q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A_{c}\\
+\frac{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}{\sqrt{a}}A_{\Gamma}\Big(a,0,\frac{\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})},\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}\Big)\Big]\\
=-a^{3/2}\rho\Big[S^{2}+q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})-A_{c}+q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big]\,.

The critical points are such that

(3.147)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\Big(1+\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)=A_{c},

(3.148)

\displaystyle S^{2}+q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\Big(1+\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)=A_{c}\,,

where
\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\Theta)=\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+2\Xi\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 2}
,
\ell(0,{\vartheta_{c}})=0
, and where homogeneous terms of order
j
come with small factors
a^{j/2}
.
We will prove that, although the determinant of the matrix of second derivatives may vanish, we can still use degenerate stationary phase with critical point of order at most
2
to conclude.
We compute the second derivative with respect to
\Sigma
using (
3.145
) :

(3.149)

\partial_{\Sigma}^{2}\phi_{N,a}=a^{3/2}\rho\Big[2\Sigma-\partial_{\Sigma}A_{c}+\frac{x}{a}\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{{\vartheta_{c}}}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},{\vartheta_{c}})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)\Big]\,,

where homogeneous terms of order
j
in
\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}
come with small factors
a^{j/2}
and

(3.150)

\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{{\vartheta_{c}}}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},{\vartheta_{c}})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)=\partial_{\Sigma}\Big(q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\\
+2\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\frac{\sqrt{a}\Sigma q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},\vartheta_{c})}+\mathcal{H}_{j\geq 2})\Big)=\partial_{\Sigma}{\vartheta_{c}}\nabla_{\vartheta}\Big(\ell(y,\vartheta)q^{1/3}(\vartheta)+2\sqrt{a}\Sigma\mu(y,\vartheta)q^{2/3}(\vartheta)+O(a)\Big)|_{\vartheta={\vartheta_{c}}}\\
+2\sqrt{a}\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})+O(a)\,.

Using that
\sqrt{a}\lesssim\frac{1}{N}
,
x\lesssim 2a
, we obtain an estimation of the second derivatives of
\phi_{N,a}

(3.151)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=a^{3/2}\rho(2\Sigma+O(a^{3/2}/|y|)+O(1/N)),

(3.152)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{S}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=a^{3/2}\rho(2S+O(a^{3/2}/|y|)+O(1/N))\,,

(3.153)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{\Sigma,S}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=O(a^{3/2}/N)\,.

We rescale variables
(\Sigma,S)=(\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x},\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y})
, so that we are left with proving

(3.154)

\left|\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}(\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x},\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y},\rho)}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x},\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y},\rho)\,d\mathbbmss{x}d\mathbbmss{y}\right|\leq{C}\,.

From the compact support of
\sigma_{V,h,a}
we obviously have, for any multi-index
\nu
,

(3.155)

|\partial^{\nu}_{(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x},\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y},\rho)|\leq C_{\nu}(1+|\mathbbmss{x}|+|\mathbbmss{y}|)^{-|\nu|}\,.

Let
\mathbb{A}_{0}
be the main term of
A_{c}^{1/2}
defined (
3.138
); we define
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})
as follows

(3.156)

-\mathfrak{A}\lambda^{-2/3}=\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2},\,\,\,\,\,-\mathfrak{B}\lambda^{-2/3}=q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}

and we also write
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})=r(\sin\mathfrak{S},\cos\mathfrak{S})
, where
r^{2}=\mathfrak{A}^{2}+\mathfrak{B}^{2}
. In the new variables, since
\frac{\partial\Sigma}{\partial\mathbbmss{x}}=\frac{\partial S}{\partial\mathbbmss{y}}=\lambda^{-1/3}
and using
\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}
as provided in (
3.150
), we find

(3.157)

\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}=\frac{a^{3/2}}{h}\lambda^{-1/3}\rho\Big[\lambda^{-2/3}(\mathbbmss{x}^{2}-\mathfrak{A})+2\frac{\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x}}{2N}\Big(\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-xE_{1})+\frac{x}{a}(N\sqrt{a})\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\Big)\\
-2\frac{\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y}}{2N}\Big(\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-aE_{2})\Big)+\frac{\lambda^{-1/3}}{N}O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N})+O(a)\Big]\\
=\rho\Big((\mathbbmss{x}^{2}-\mathfrak{A})+\frac{\lambda^{1/3}}{N}\Big(\mathbbmss{x}(\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-xE_{1})+O(N\sqrt{a}))-\mathbbmss{y}\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-aE_{2})+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N}))+O(\lambda^{2/3}a)\Big)\\
=\rho(\mathbbmss{x}^{2}-\mathfrak{A}+O(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})+O(1))\,,

where the term
O(a)
in the second comes from the terms homogeneous of order
j\geq 2
in the term
\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{{\vartheta_{c}}}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},{\vartheta_{c}})})|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{a}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(aA_{c},\vartheta_{c})}}
(see (
3.150
)). We have written
O(\lambda^{2/3}a)=O((\lambda^{1/3}/N\times N\sqrt{a})^{2})=O(1)
since
{\lambda^{1/3}}/{N}<1
and
(Na^{1/2})<1
.
Similarly, derivatives with respect to
\mathbbmss{x}
and
\mathbbmss{y}
are,

(3.158)

\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}=\rho(\mathbbmss{x}^{2}-\mathfrak{A}+O(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})+O(1))\,,\quad\quad\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}=\rho(\mathbbmss{y}^{2}-\mathfrak{B}+O(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})+O(1))\,.

From (
3.151
), we obtain, in the new coordinates

(3.159)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}^{2}\phi_{N,a}=\lambda^{1/3}\rho(2\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x}+O(N^{-1}))=\rho(2\mathbbmss{x}+O(1))\,,

(3.160)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}^{2}\phi_{N,a}=\lambda^{1/3}\rho(2\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y}+O(N^{-1}))=\rho(2\mathbbmss{y}+O(1))\,,

where we used
|N|>\lambda^{1/3}
in the last step together with
|y|\geq c_{0}t
which yields
a^{3/2}/|y|\lesssim a^{3/2}/t\sim a/N
, as
t/\sqrt{a}\sim 4N
when the phase is stationary in
A
.
Now, going back to (
3.154
), the integral is bounded for
0\leq r\leq r_{0}
, for some
r_{0}>0
, by integration by parts for large
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
(recall that the support of the integrand is now of radius
\lambda^{1/3}
). For
r_{0}<r\lesssim\lambda^{2/3}
, set
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=r^{1/2}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})
,
\phi_{N,a}=r^{3/2}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},.)=\sigma_{V,h,a}(r^{1/2}\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},r^{1/2}\lambda^{-1/3}\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},\rho)
, and as
r^{1/2}\lambda^{-1/3}
is bounded, we retain the decay

|\partial^{\nu}_{(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},.)|\leq C_{\nu}(1+|\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}|+|\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}|)^{-|\nu|}
.
It remains to prove

(3.161)

r|\int e^{i\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}\,d\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}|\leq C\,.

To begin with, notice that

(3.162)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}}(\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a})

\displaystyle=r^{3/2}\rho\Big(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime 2}-\sin\mathfrak{S}+r^{-1/2}O(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})+r^{-1}O(1)\Big)\,,

(3.163)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}}(\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a})

\displaystyle=r^{3/2}\rho\Big(\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime 2}-\cos\mathfrak{S}+r^{-1/2}O(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})+r^{-1}O(1)\Big)\,.

If
|\sin\mathfrak{S}|\geq 1/100
, then
\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}
has two critical points in
\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}
, namely
\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}_{\pm}=\pm|\sin\mathfrak{S}|^{1/2}+O(r^{-1/2})
,
and these critical points are non degenerate as

|\partial^{2}_{\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a})|\geq\rho/10
. By stationary phase, we get

(3.164)

\int e^{i\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}\,d\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}=(r^{3/2})^{-1/2}(\int e^{i\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,h,a,+}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a,+}\,d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}+\int e^{i\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a,-}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a,-}\,d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})\,,

for some symbols
\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a,\pm}
of order
0
.
Using (
3.159
), one may check that
|\partial^{3}_{\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a})|\geq c>0
, and by degenerate stationary phase (or Van der Corput lemma) we get

(3.165)

|\int e^{i\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a,\pm}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a,\pm}\,d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}|\leq C(r^{3/2})^{-1/3}=Cr^{-1/2}\,,

and for
r\geq 1
, we get the desired decay, and even an extra
r^{-1/4}
on the right hand side. If
|\cos\mathfrak{S}|\geq 1/100
, we proceed in the same way, exchanging
\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}
and
\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}
.

Let us now deal with
|N|\geq\lambda^{2}
. Stationary phase applies in
\rho
and from (
3.143
),

(3.166)

\frac{1}{h}|\partial^{2}_{\rho}\phi_{N,a}|\sim\frac{|N|}{\lambda}\,,

hence the stationary phase in
\rho
yields a factor
(\frac{|N|}{\lambda})^{-1/2}
. In the following we apply exactly the same method as in the previous step; the only difference is that now we have an additional function
\rho_{c}
which depends on the variables
\Sigma,S
and whose derivatives with respect to these variables are small only for
|N|>\lambda^{2}
(which explains why we did not perform stationary phase with respect to
\rho
earlier, for any
|N|>\lambda^{1/3}
). Indeed, from
\partial_{\rho}\phi_{N,a}=\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,a}|_{A_{c}}=0
we get

(3.167)

\Psi_{0,a,a}-\frac{4}{3}a^{3/2}NA_{c}^{3/2}=Nh\lambda A_{c}^{3/2}B^{\prime}_{L}(\rho\lambda A_{c}^{3/2})=-\frac{NhA_{c}^{3/2}}{\rho^{2}\lambda A_{c}^{3}}(b_{1}+O(1/\lambda)),\quad b_{1}\neq 0\,,

where the term in the left hand side doesn’t depend on
\rho
. Since
\rho=|\theta|\sim 1
and since the support of the symbol in
A_{c}
is a given compact set of
(0,\infty)
, we have

\rho^{2}_{c}=-{\frac{Nh}{\lambda A_{c}^{3/2}}(b_{1}+O(1/\lambda))}\Bigl({\Psi_{0,a,a}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,1)-\frac{4}{3}a^{3/2}NA_{c}^{3/2}}\Bigr)^{-1}\,.

Taking the derivatives with respect to
S,\Upsilon
of (
3.167
), with
A_{c}
bounded, provides a factor
\lambda^{2}/N
which is small in the regime we consider here,
|N|>C\lambda^{2}
for some
C>1
sufficiently large (indeed, the terms containing
\Sigma,S
in (
3.167
) come with a factor
a^{3/2}=h\lambda
). Since
\partial_{\Sigma}\rho_{c}
and
\partial_{S}\rho_{c}
are now sufficiently small, we can follow the same earlier steps to estimate the integral in the remaining variables
\Sigma,S
.
∎

3.2.2.
Moderately large
0<|N|\leq\lambda^{1/3}

In this case the contribution from the integral in
\rho
is uniformly bounded due to its compact support. According to Remark
3.25
, we bring the factor
e^{iNB_{L}}
into the symbol and work with the phase
\Psi_{0,a,a}-\frac{4}{3}a^{3/2}\rho NA^{3/2}
, which is linear in
\rho
. Therefore, the critical point
A_{c}
satisfies an equation similar to (
3.139
), but without the factor
1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda A^{3/2})
, which leads to an explicit expression of the form (
3.137
) where
f_{0}=0
. We start in the same way as in the proof of Lemma
3.30
, replacing
\lambda^{1/3}
by
|N|
: we rescale variables with
(\Sigma,S)=(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{|N|},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{|N|})
.

Lemma 3.32
.

For
|N|\geq 1
, set
\Lambda=\lambda|N|^{-3}
and assume
\Lambda\geq 1
, then we have

(3.168)

\left|\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{|N|},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{|N|},\rho)\,d\mathbbmss{x}d\mathbbmss{y}\right|\leq C\Lambda^{-3/4}\,.

Proof.

Here we need all the first three terms in the formula (
3.137
). In our new variables,
\mathbb{A}_{0}
, as defined in (
3.138
), does not change and
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\frac{1}{2N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}(1-xE_{1})-\mathbbmss{y}(1-aE_{2}))
, hence, using (
3.137
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle\!\begin{multlined}=\Lambda r\Big(\mathbbmss{x}^{2}+N^{2}(\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}))+\mathbb{A}_{0}\Big(\mathbbmss{x}(1-xE_{1})-\mathbbmss{y}(1-aE_{2})\Big)\\
+2N\sqrt{a}\mathbbmss{x}\frac{x}{a}\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})-\frac{1}{4N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}})+N^{2}O(a)\Big),\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle\!\begin{multlined}=-\Lambda r\Big(\mathbbmss{y}^{2}+N^{2}(q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2})+\mathbb{A}_{0}\Big(\mathbbmss{x}(1-xE_{1})-\mathbbmss{y}(1-aE_{2})\Big)\\
-2N\sqrt{a}\mathbbmss{y}\mu(0,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})-\frac{1}{4N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}})+N^{2}O(a)\Big),\end{multlined}

where
N^{2}O(a)
in the last two formulas are homogeneous terms of order
j\geq 2
in the expression of
\partial_{\Xi}A_{\Gamma}
.
Next, we compute the second derivatives : notice that (
3.134
) yields
|\nabla_{\Sigma,S}{\vartheta_{c}}|\sim O(a^{3/2}/|y|)\sim O(a/N)
since
|y|\geq c_{0}t
and
t/\sqrt{a}\sim 4N
.
Using (
3.149
) and (
3.138
) we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial^{2}_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=\Lambda\rho\Big(2\mathbbmss{x}+2N\sqrt{a}\frac{x}{a}\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})+\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-xE_{1})-\frac{1}{2N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N^{2}})\Big),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial^{2}_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=-\Lambda\rho\Big(2\mathbbmss{y}+2N\sqrt{a}\mu(0,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+O(\sqrt{a}))+\mathbb{A}_{0}(1-aE_{2})-\frac{1}{2N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})\\
{}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}})\Big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=\Lambda\rho\Big(\mathbb{A}_{0}-\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})}{2N^{2}}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N^{2}})\Big)\,.

In fact, we infer that we can write

(3.173)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}=\Lambda\rho\Big(\mathbbmss{x}^{2}-\mathfrak{A}+(b\mathbbmss{x}-d\mathbbmss{y})-\frac{1}{4N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}})\Big),

(3.174)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}=-\Lambda\rho\Big(\mathbbmss{y}^{2}-\mathfrak{B}+(d\mathbbmss{x}-c\mathbbmss{y})-\frac{1}{4N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}})\Big),

where
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})
and
b
,
c
,
d
are defined as follows

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathfrak{A}}{N^{2}}=\frac{x}{a}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+\ell(y,{\vartheta_{c}}))-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}\,,\quad-\frac{\mathfrak{B}}{N^{2}}=q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})-\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} b&=\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(x))+N\sqrt{a}\frac{x}{a}\mu(y,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+O(\sqrt{a}))\,,\\
c&=\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(a))-N\sqrt{a}\mu(0,{\vartheta_{c}})q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})(1+O(\sqrt{a}))\\
d&=\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(x;a))\,.\end{aligned}

This follows easily from writing the Taylor development for the first order derivatives of
\phi_{N,a}
with respect to
\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y}
and use that
\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}=\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}
. We also have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial^{2}_{\mathbbmss{x}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=\Lambda\rho\Big(2\mathbbmss{x}+b-\frac{1}{2N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}^{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N^{2}})\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial^{2}_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda\rho\Big(2\mathbbmss{y}-c+\frac{1}{2N^{2}}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}^{2}}{N^{2}})\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}}\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}}\phi_{N,a}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda\rho\Big(d-\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})}{2N^{2}}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N^{2}})\Big)\,.

Let
\Lambda\rho M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
denote the matrix of second order derivatives (i.e. the Hessian of
\frac{1}{h}\phi_{N,a}
); we compute its determinant

(3.175)

\det M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=-\Lambda^{2}\rho^{2}\Big(4\mathbbmss{x}\mathbbmss{y}+2(b\mathbbmss{y}-c\mathbbmss{x})+d^{2}-bc+\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}}{N^{2}}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{N^{2}})\Big)\,.

Set
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})=r(\sin\mathfrak{S},\cos\mathfrak{S})
. We will again deal separately with
r\geq r_{0}
, for some large
r_{0}>1
, and then
r<r_{0}
. We start with large
r\geq r_{0}>1
and we prove

(3.176)

\left|\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{|N|},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{|N|})\,d\mathbbmss{x}d\mathbbmss{y}\right|\leq C\Lambda^{-5/6}\,,

which has better decay than required. First, observe that from the hypothesis on
r
and (
3.173
), we may integrate by parts in
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
in a region
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\leq cr^{1/2}
with
c
small enough: for any
k\geq 1
,

(3.177)

\left|\int_{|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})\leq cr^{1/2}}e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{|N|},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{|N|})\,d\mathbbmss{x}d\mathbbmss{y}\right|\leq C_{k}(r\Lambda)^{-k}\,.

Thus we are left with the region
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\geq cr^{1/2}
. We rescale again
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=r^{1/2}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})
and, in the new variables, we prove the following

(3.178)

\left|r\int_{|(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})\geq c}e^{\frac{i}{h}r^{3/2}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}}\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},.)\,d\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}\right|\leq C\Lambda^{-5/6}\,,

where we set, like in the previous section,
\phi_{N,a}(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y},.)=r^{3/2}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},.)
and
\tilde{\sigma}_{V,h,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})=\sigma_{V,h,a}(r^{1/2}\mathbbmss{x}/N,r^{1/2}\mathbbmss{y}/N,.)
.
Taking
b=r^{1/2}b^{\prime}
,
d=r^{1/2}d^{\prime}
and
c=r^{1/2}c^{\prime}
, we have

(3.179)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Lambda\rho r^{3/2}\Big(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime 2}-\sin\mathfrak{S}+2(b^{\prime}\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}-d^{\prime}\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})-\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}-\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})^{2}}{4N^{2}}+O(1/N^{2})\Big)

(3.180)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}\Big)

\displaystyle=-\Lambda\rho r^{3/2}\Big(\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime 2}-\cos\mathfrak{S}+2(d^{\prime}\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}-c^{\prime}\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})-\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}-\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})^{2}}{4N^{2}}+O(1/N^{2})\Big)\,.

From these expressions of
\nabla_{(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})}(\frac{r^{3/2}}{h}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a})
, if
(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})

is large, we get decay by integrations by parts, as
|N|\geq 2
. Therefore, we
can assume that
c\leq|(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})|\leq C
where
C
is a large, fixed
constant (we avoid a neighbourhood of
(0,0)
since we have assumed
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|>cr^{1/2}
).
If
|N|\geq 2
,
a
is small and
r_{0}
is large, the set
\{\det M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=0\}
is a smooth curve which does not intersect the origin. Away from this set, we may use stationary phase, which will provide
r(r^{3/2}\Lambda)^{-1}
decay on the left of (
3.178
). In the region close to
\{\det M(r^{1/2}\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},r^{1/2}\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})=0\}
, we can apply
[
9
, Lemma 2.21 (a)]
, i.e. degenerate stationary phase along a curve, to obtain

(3.181)

\left|\int_{c\leq|(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime})\leq C}e^{\frac{i}{h}r^{3/2}\tilde{\phi}_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime},\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime},.)\,d\mathbbmss{x}^{\prime}d\mathbbmss{y}^{\prime}\right|\leq C(r^{3/2}\Lambda)^{-5/6}\,,

and therefore we get (
3.178
) as the extra factor
r
on the lefthand side is canceled by the
r^{-5/4}
on the righthand side (recall
r\geq r_{0}>1
).

We can now focus on
r=|(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})|\leq r_{0}
. Notice that we may further restrict support again, this time to
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\leq 2r^{1/2}\leq 2r^{1/2}_{0}
as we get
\Lambda^{-\infty}
decay by integration by parts if
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|
is larger than this value, as we did before. We now aim at proving

(3.182)

\left|\int_{|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\leq 2r^{1/2}}e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a}}\sigma_{V,h,a}(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}}{|N|},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}}{|N|})\,d\mathbbmss{x}d\mathbbmss{y}\right|\leq C\Lambda^{-3/4}\,,

for which we will apply

[
9
, Lemma 2.21 (b)]
. The determinant of the Hessian
M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
of

\frac{1}{h}\phi_{N,a}
is given in (
3.175
) and the set
\{\det M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=0\}
is a smooth curve in

\{|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\leq 2r^{1/2}\}
, at least for small
a
: it will be close to
(2\mathbbmss{x}+b)(2\mathbbmss{y}-c)+d^{2}=-(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}
when
|N|=1
and
(2\mathbbmss{x}+b)(2\mathbbmss{y}-c)+d^{2}=-(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}/N^{2}
for

|N|\geq 2
(and using
b\sim c\sim d\sim\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(a))
, we see that it is close to the hyperbola
4\mathbbmss{x}\mathbbmss{y}+2\mathbb{A}_{0}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})=-(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})^{2}/N^{2}
for
|N|>1
and to the parabola
2\mathbb{A}_{0}(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{y})=-(\mathbbmss{x}+\mathbbmss{y})^{2}
when
|N|=1
).

Let
(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
such that
|(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})|\leq 2r^{1/2}
: if
\det M(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})\neq 0
, then
the usual stationary phase applies, unless we are in the special
condition where
[
9
, Lemma 2.21]
takes over and we recall it now: let
H(\xi)
be a smooth function defined in a neighborhood of
(0,0)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, such that
H(0)=0
and
\nabla H(0)=0
.
We assume that the Hessian
H^{\prime\prime}
satisfies
\text{rank}(H^{\prime\prime}(0))=1
and
\nabla\det(H^{\prime\prime})(0)\not=0
.
Then
\det(H^{\prime\prime})(q,p)=0
defines a smooth curve
\mathcal{C}
near
0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with
0\in\mathcal{C}
.
Let
s\rightarrow\xi(s)
be a smooth parametrization of
\mathcal{C}
, with
\xi(0)=0
, and define the curve
\Xi(s):=H^{\prime}(\xi(s))
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.33
.

(
[
9
, Lemma 2.21]
)
Let
K=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2},|\xi|\leq R_{0}\}
, and
a(\xi,\Lambda)
a classical symbol of
order
0
in
\Lambda\geq 1
with
a(\xi,\Lambda)=0
for
\xi\notin K
.
Set for
(P,Q)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
close to
0

(3.183)

I(\cdot,\Lambda):=\int e^{i\Lambda(<\xi,\cdot>-H(\xi))}a(\xi,\Lambda)d\xi\,.

Then for
R_{0}>0
small enough, the following holds true:

(a)

If
\Xi^{\prime}(0)\not=0
, there exists
C
such that for all
(P,Q)
close to
0
,
|I(\cdot,\Lambda)|\leq C\Lambda^{-5/6}
.

(b)

If
\Xi^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\Xi^{\prime\prime}(0)\not=0

there exists
C
such that for all
\cdot
close to
0
,
|I(\cdot,\Lambda)|\leq C\Lambda^{-3/4}
.
Moreover, if
a
is elliptic at
\xi=0
, there exists
C^{\prime}
such that
|I((0,0),\Lambda)|\geq C^{\prime}\Lambda^{-3/4}
.

Let
(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
be such
|(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})|\leq 2r_{0}^{1/2}
and
\det M(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})=0
.
For
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
near
(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
,
|(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})|\leq 2r^{1/2}_{0}
let
\xi=(\mathbbmss{x}-\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}-\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
and let
R_{0}=4r_{0}^{1/2}
, then
|\xi|\leq R_{0}
.
We set

H(\xi)=\Phi_{N}(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})-\Phi_{N}(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})-\nabla\phi_{N,a}(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})\cdot\xi,\quad(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})+\xi.

We see that
H(0)=0
,
H^{\prime}(0)=0
and
H^{\prime\prime}(0)=\nabla^{2}\phi_{N,a}(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})=M(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
. The matrix
M
has two
eigenvalues,
0
with normalized eigenvector

v(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
and
\lambda_{1}=\text{tr}(M(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0}))
with
normalized eigenvector
u(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})
. Let
Q
be the
matrix formed with column vectors
u
and
v
, then
{}^{t}QM(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})Q=\text{diag}(\lambda_{1}/2,0)
, and
\lambda_{1}\neq 0

as the rank of
M(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})\geq 1
(thanks to
|N|\geq 2
and

(t,x,y)
close to
0
). Using (
3.175
), a simple computation yields

(3.184)

|\nabla\text{det}H^{\prime\prime}(0)|^{2}=16\Big(\mathbbmss{x}_{0}+b/2-\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}_{0}-\mathbbmss{y}_{0})}{2N^{2}}\Big)^{2}+16\Big(\mathbbmss{y}_{0}-c/2+\frac{(\mathbbmss{x}_{0}-\mathbbmss{y}_{0})}{2N^{2}}\Big)^{2}+O(\frac{\mathbbmss{x}_{0}}{N^{2}},\frac{\mathbbmss{y}_{0}}{N^{2}}).

We need to prove that there exists a positive constant
C_{0}>0
such that

|\nabla\text{det}H^{\prime\prime}(0)|^{2}\geq C_{0}>0
. Since
b=\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(a))+O(a^{1/2}N)
,
c=\mathbb{A}_{0}(1+O(a))+O(a^{1/2}N)
it follows that for
(\mathbbmss{x}_{0},\mathbbmss{y}_{0})\in\{M(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})=0\}
we can write

|\nabla\text{det}H^{\prime\prime}(0)|^{2}=8\mathbb{A}_{0}^{2}+16(\mathbbmss{x}_{0}-\mathbbmss{y}_{0})^{2}(1-\frac{1}{2N^{2}})+O(a^{1/2}N)
,
provided
a^{1/2}N
is small
enough and therefore
|\nabla\text{det}H^{\prime\prime}(0)|\geq C_{0}>0
for some positive constant
C_{0}
.
This is enough to apply Lemma
3.33
(case [b]) and get the desired bounds.
When
N=1
, one may inspect the previous proofs to check that, knowing from support conditions that
(\mathbbmss{x},\mathbbmss{y})
is bounded, for large
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})
integrations by parts provide decay. On the other hand, in the range
(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B})
bounded, one may proceed as before, using Lemma
3.33
.
∎

We moreover remark that the last statement in Lemma
3.33
, together with ellipticity of the symbol
\sigma_{V,h,a}
, provides, at fixed
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
, a sequence
(t_{N},x_{N},y_{N})
where the bound (
3.168
) saturates, which is exactly at the swallowtail singularity in space-time. This is a key point in proving Theorem
1.3
: for now, we have
V_{N}(t_{N},x_{N},y_{N})\sim\|V_{N}\|_{{\infty}}
, and at
(t_{N},x_{N},y_{N})
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\sim\mathfrak{B}\sim 0
, i.e.
\mathbb{A}_{0}\sim q^{1/6}(\theta)
, implying
t_{N}\sim 4Nq^{-1/2}(y/|y|)\sqrt{a}
,
x_{N}\sim a
and
y_{N}/|y_{N}\sim\vartheta_{c}\sim\theta
. Moreover, at
(t_{M},x_{N},y_{N})
with
M\neq N
and
|M-N|\leq M_{0}
,
\mathfrak{B}\sim CN
and
|V_{N}(t_{M},x_{N},y_{N})|
decays much faster (
\Lambda^{-5/6}
rather than
\Lambda^{-3/4}
). Therefore when summing over
M
at fixed
N
, only
V_{N}
contributes to saturating the bound and Theorem
1.3
holds.

3.2.3.
The sum over
2\leq N\lesssim\sqrt{a}
: completing the proof of Proposition
3.23

We now prove (
3.131
).
For
N\not\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
, Proposition
3.13
provides an
O_{C^{\infty}}(h^{\infty})
contribution. Recall that if
(\theta=(\rho,\vartheta),A,\Sigma,S)
is a critical point
in the phase integral defining
V_{N,a}
, then
t/(4\sqrt{a}|N|)\sim A^{1/2}
and on the support of the symbol
\psi(A)
we have
A\sim 1
. Set
\sharp N=|\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)|
. If
\sharp N\leq 2C
, the sum reduces to a finite sum, discarding all
N\not\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(x,y,t)
. For those
N\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
, if
|N|\leq\lambda^{1/3}
, then, collecting (
3.133
) (stationary phase in
\vartheta
), (
3.136
) (stationary phase in
A
) and (
3.168
) (degenerate stationary phase in
(\Sigma,S)
),

(3.185)

|V_{N,a}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{a^{2}}{h}\frac{1}{|N|^{1/2}\lambda^{1/2}}|N|^{1/4}\lambda^{-3/4}

from which, with
t\sim 4a^{1/2}N
we get the desired result:

(3.186)

|V_{N,a}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{a^{1/8}h^{1/4}}{|N|^{1/4}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{a^{1/4}h^{1/4}}{t^{1/4}}\,.

If
|N|\geq\lambda^{1/3}
, we collect the same bounds but with (
3.168
) replaced by (
3.144
):

(3.187)

|V_{N,a}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{a^{2}}{h}\frac{1}{|N|^{1/2}\lambda^{1/2}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2/3}}

and one easily checks that
a^{1/2}N^{-1/2}\lambda^{-1/6}\leq a^{1/8}h^{1/4}N^{-1/4}
is equivalent to
|N|\geq\lambda^{1/3}
(using
1/N\leq\lambda^{-1/3}
we get
h^{1/3}
instead of
a^{1/8}h^{1/4}/|N|^{1/4}
.) Moreover, if only a finite number of
V_{N,a}(t,x,y)
contributes, we have
a>h^{4/7}
.
We proceed with large
\sharp N(\geq 2C)
: as
\sharp N\leq C+C\frac{t}{\sqrt{a}\lambda^{2}}
, we have
t\geq c\sqrt{a}\lambda^{2}
. We also have
|N|\sim t/(4\sqrt{a})
and from
t\lesssim 1
, we have
a\lesssim h^{4/7}
.
For those
N
,

(3.188)

\left|\sum_{N\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y),|N|\sim t/\sqrt{a}}V_{N,a}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}a^{1/2}\lambda^{1/3}\frac{1}{|N|}\sharp N\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}h^{1/3}\lambda^{-4/3}\,,

and one checks that
h^{1/3}\lambda^{-4/3}\leq h^{1/3}
for
a\gtrsim h^{2/3}
. This completes the proof of Proposition
3.23
.

3.3.
The transverse part
4a\leq\gamma\ll 1

We go back to
\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)
, which is the critical value of
\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
after the stationary phase in
\vartheta
: when
4a\leq\gamma
we start by applying stationary phase in
S
, as in the proof of Proposition
3.13
.
The phase has two distinct saddle points
S_{\pm}
,

(3.189)

S_{\pm}^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})\Big(1+\partial_{\Xi}\Big(A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta_{c})})\Big)|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}S_{\pm}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)=A.

Using (
3.134
),
{\vartheta_{c}}
(function of
\Sigma,S,A
) does not affect the second derivative in
S
significantly and we get
\partial^{2}_{S}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}|_{S_{\pm}}\sim 2
. Moreover, the critical points
S_{\pm}
depend on
\Sigma
only through
{\vartheta_{c}}
and we have
S_{\pm}=S_{0}\pm\sqrt{A-O(a/\gamma)}
, where
S_{0}
is the unique solution to
\partial^{2}_{S}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)=0
and it satisfies
S_{0}=O(a/\sqrt{\gamma})
(indeed, from Corollary
6.10
, the unique solution
s_{0}
to
\partial^{2}_{s}\tilde{\Phi}_{N,a,\gamma}
satisfies
s_{0}=O(a)
; making the change of variables
s=\sqrt{\gamma}\rho^{1/3}S
gives
S_{0}=O(a/\sqrt{\gamma})
). We are left with an integral with respect to
A,\Sigma,\rho
and we pick a factor
\lambda_{\gamma}^{1/2}
.

When
1\leq|N|\lesssim\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
, we ignore
\rho
: we first perform stationary phase with respect to
A
, and then prove that the remaining phase has critical points that may be degenerate of order at most two in
\Sigma
. For each
S_{\pm}
, the critical point with respect to
A
, denoted
A_{\pm}
, is such that, with
\tau=\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta_{c})
,

(3.190)

2NA^{1/2}(1-\frac{3}{4}B_{L}^{\prime}(\rho\lambda_{\gamma}A^{3/2}))=\frac{q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})\Big(t-B_{2}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\frac{(1-\tau)^{k}}{\tau^{k-1}}\Big)B_{2k}(y,{\vartheta_{c}})\Big)}{2\sqrt{\gamma}\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\\
{}-\Sigma+S_{\pm}+\frac{{\gamma}q^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}{\sqrt{1+\gamma Aq^{2/3}({\vartheta_{c}})}}\Big[\frac{x}{\gamma^{3/2}}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\sqrt{\gamma}\Sigma q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)\\
{}-\frac{a}{\gamma^{3/2}}\partial_{\tau}\Big(\tau A_{\Gamma}(a,0,\sqrt{\gamma}S_{\pm}q^{1/3}({\vartheta_{c}})/\tau,{\vartheta_{c}}/\tau)\Big)\Big].

The second derivative with respect to
A
behaves like
N/\sqrt{A}
and with
A\sim 1
on the support of the symbol
\psi
, stationary phase provides a factor
\lambda_{\gamma}^{-1/2}|N|^{-1/2}
.

When
|N|>\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
we apply stationary phase in
A
just like we did, and then stationary phase in
\rho
. We then prove that the remaining integral can be degenerate of order at most two. To apply (additional) stationary phase in
\rho
we act exactly like in the case
\gamma\sim a
and obtain, as in (
3.166
)

\frac{1}{h}|\partial^{2}_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{+},A_{+},\rho)|\sim\frac{1}{h}|\partial^{2}_{\rho}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{+},A_{+},\rho)|\sim\frac{|N|}{\lambda_{\gamma}}\,.

Let
\phi_{N,a,\gamma,+}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho)=\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{+},A_{+},\rho)
and, respectively,
\phi_{N,a,\gamma,-}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho)=\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S_{-},A_{-},\rho)
, denote the critical values of
\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}
after the the stationary phase in
S
and then in
A
(notice that
A_{+}
(and
A_{-}
, respectively) is the critical point of the phase
\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,S_{+},A,\rho)
(and
\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,S_{-},A,\rho)
, respectively), so the critical points
S
and
A
are paired with same sign, either
+
or
-
).
Let
\sigma_{V,h,\gamma,\pm}(\Sigma,\rho)
be the symbol obtained from

\psi(A)p_{h}(x,y,\theta,\gamma|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}\Sigma)\tilde{q}_{h}(\theta,\gamma|\theta|^{2/3}A,\sqrt{\gamma}|\theta|^{1/3}S)

after applying stationary phase in
\vartheta
,
A
and
S
, evaluated at
(S,A)_{\pm}
;
\sigma_{V,h,\gamma}
is independent of
N
, of order zero and has compact support in
\Sigma
. We are left with estimating

\int e^{\frac{i}{h}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}}\sigma_{V,h,\gamma,\pm}d\Sigma d\rho.

Following
[
12
]
, we state different estimates for
|N|\geq 2
,
N=0
and
N=\pm 1
.

Lemma 3.34
.

For
|N|\geq 2
and a given point
(t,x,y)
with
t\sim 4N\sqrt{\gamma}
, the phase
\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho)
has at most one degenerate critical point, which is of order two.

Proof.

The first derivative of the phase
\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}
is

(3.191)

\displaystyle\partial_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\Sigma}\Psi_{N,a,\gamma}(t,x,y,\Sigma,S,A,\rho)|_{A_{\pm},S_{\pm}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{2}+\frac{x}{\gamma}q^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})\Big(1+\partial_{\Xi}\Big(A_{\Gamma}(x,y,\Xi,\frac{\vartheta_{c}}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta_{c})})\Big)|_{\Xi=\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}Sq^{1/3}(\vartheta_{c})}{\tau_{q}(\gamma A,\vartheta_{c})}}\Big)-A_{c}

Using (
3.190
) and then (
3.191
), we estimate derivatives w.r.t.
\Sigma
as follows

(3.192)

\displaystyle\partial_{\Sigma}(A^{1/2}_{\pm})=-\frac{1}{2N\mp 1}(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma})+O(\lambda^{-2})+O(\partial_{\Sigma}\omega_{c}))=-\frac{1}{2N\mp 1}(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))\,,

(3.193)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}=2\Sigma(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))+\frac{A_{\pm}^{1/2}}{2N\mp 1}(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))\,,\quad\partial^{3}_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}=2+O(1/N)+O(\sqrt{\gamma})\,.

For a given
t,x,y,N
, the equation
\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}=0
has at most one solution which is a saddle point when
\partial_{\Sigma}\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}=0
. Since the third order derivative stays close to
2
, degenerate stationary phase (or Van der Corput Lemma) in
\Sigma
provides a factor
\lambda_{\gamma}^{-1/3}
.
∎

Lemma 3.35
.

Let
N=0
,
|t|>\gamma
, then for
|\Sigma|\lesssim 1
we have
\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{0,a,\gamma,\pm}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho)\sim t/\sqrt{\gamma}
.

Proof.

Let
N=0
,
|t|>\gamma
, then
A_{\pm}
solve (
3.190
) where the term in the left hand side vanishes. The main term containing
A
comes from
S_{\pm}=S_{0}\pm\sqrt{A-O(a/\gamma)}
and
A_{\pm}
satisfy

(3.194)

S_{0}\pm\sqrt{A_{\pm}-O(a/\gamma)}=\Sigma(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})\frac{(t+O(|y|))}{2\sqrt{\gamma}}(1+O(\gamma)).

Taking the derivative of (
3.194
) with respect to
\Sigma
, using that
S_{0}
depends on
\Sigma
only through
\vartheta_{c}
(which satisfies (
3.134
)), that
A_{\pm}
stays close to
1
and that
t/\sqrt{\gamma}
is bounded yields

\displaystyle\partial_{\Sigma}A_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm 2\sqrt{A_{\pm}-O(a/\gamma)}+O(\sqrt{\gamma})+O(\gamma^{3/2}/|y|)\times\frac{(t+O(|y|))}{2\sqrt{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=2\Big(\Sigma(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})\frac{(t+O(|y|))}{2\sqrt{\gamma}}(1+O(\gamma))\Big)+O(\sqrt{\gamma}),

and therefore, using that the saddle point
\Sigma
is bounded since
\Sigma^{2}\leq A_{\pm}
, we find

\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{0,a,\gamma,\pm}|_{\partial_{\Sigma}\phi_{0,a,\gamma,\pm}=0}=2\Sigma(1+O(\sqrt{\gamma}))-\partial_{\Sigma}A_{\pm}=q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{c})\frac{(t+O(|y|))}{\sqrt{\gamma}}(1+O(\gamma))+O(\sqrt{\gamma})\,.

When
|t|>\gamma
we obtain the result. When
|t|\lesssim\gamma
, the wave has no time to reach the boundary.
∎

Lemma 3.36
.

For
N=\pm 1
: each phase function
\phi_{\pm 1,a,\gamma,\pm}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho)
has at most one degenerate critical point
\Sigma_{c}
of order exactly two; for
t\neq 0
, the equation
\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{\pm 1,a,\gamma,\mp}=0
has an unique solution
\Sigma\sim\mp 1/\gamma
, while for
|\Sigma|\lesssim 1
, we have
\partial^{2}_{\Sigma}\phi_{\pm 1,a,\gamma,\mp}\sim(t/\sqrt{\gamma})(1+O(\gamma))
.

Proof.

For
\phi_{\pm 1,a,\gamma,\pm}
, the proof of Lemma
3.34
applies, for
\phi_{\pm 1,a,\gamma,\mp}
the proof for
N=0
does.
∎

Lemma 3.37
.

For
|N|\geq\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
, after stationary phase in
\rho
, the critical value
\phi_{N,a,\gamma,\pm}(t,x,y,\Sigma,\rho_{c})
has at most one degenerate critical point of order exactly two in
\Sigma
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as the one of Lemma
3.34
since the contribution from the derivatives of the critical point with respect to
\rho
do not affect significantly the third order derivative of the phase with respect to
\Sigma
.
∎

3.3.1.
Estimates for the sum over
N
and end of the proof of Proposition
3.22

We now proceed with estimating the sum over
N
, i.e. proving (
3.130
).
Again, for
N\not\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
, Proposition
3.13
provides an
O_{C^{\infty}}(h^{\infty})
contribution. With
\sharp N=|\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)|
, if
\sharp N\leq 2C
, the sum reduces to a finite sum. For those
N\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)
, we have
|N|\leq\lambda^{2}
, then, collecting (
3.133
) (stationary phase in
\vartheta
), factors from stationary phase in
S,A
and from degenerate stationary phase in
\Sigma
,

(3.195)

|V_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{h}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\gamma}^{1/2}}\frac{1}{|N|^{1/2}\lambda^{1/2}_{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/3}_{\gamma}}\sim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{h^{1/3}}{\sqrt{N}}\,.

If
|N|\geq\lambda_{\gamma}^{2}
, we collect the same bounds but with an additional stationary phase in
\rho
,

(3.196)

|V_{N,\gamma}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{h}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\gamma}^{1/2}}\frac{\lambda^{1/2}_{\gamma}}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{1}{|N|^{1/2}\lambda^{1/2}_{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/3}_{\gamma}}\sim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{h^{1/3}\lambda^{1/2}_{\gamma}}{N}\,.

As
\sharp N\leq C+C\frac{T}{\lambda^{2}}
and
T\sim N
, we complete the proof of Proposition
3.22
with

(3.197)

\left|\sum_{N\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(t,x,y)}V_{N,\gamma}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{h^{1/3}\lambda^{1/2}_{\gamma}}{N}\sharp N\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left(\frac{h}{t}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\frac{h^{1/3}}{\lambda^{3/2}_{\gamma}}\,.

Remark 3.38
.

In this transverse regime, we used
\gamma\geq h^{2/3-\varepsilon}
rather than
a\geq h^{2/3-\varepsilon}
. As such, all estimates hold with any
a>0
. This will be of importance in the next section.

4.
Dispersion for small
a\lesssim h^{2/3-\epsilon}

We now obtain dispersion for
\mathcal{P}_{h,a}
for small
a\in(0,h^{2/3-\epsilon})
with a small
0<\epsilon<1/12
using Propositions
2.18
and
2.19
. Write

\mathcal{P}_{h,a}(t,x,y):=\mathcal{P}^{1}_{h,a}+\mathcal{P}^{2}_{h,a}\,,

where
\mathcal{P}^{j}_{h,a}
is given by (
2.51
) with
g_{h,a}
replaced by
g_{h,a,j}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
. Dispersion for
\mathcal{P}^{2}_{h,a}
easily follows using exactly the arguments from the transverse case (for
a>h^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
):
\omega
is large and stationary phase arguments apply. We are left with
\mathcal{P}^{1}_{h,a}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

For
h<t\leq T_{0}\leq 1

(4.1)

\|\mathcal{P}^{1}_{h,a}(t,.)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x>0,y}}\lesssim h^{-d}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{1/3}.

Proof.

We need the following lemma, relying on Corollary
2.31
:

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists symbols
\sigma(\eta,\omega_{k})

and
r_{j}(a,\eta,\omega_{k})
, of order
0
,
\sigma,r_{0}
elliptic such that

(4.2)

K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{h,a,1})(t,x,y)=\int e^{it\tau_{q}(\omega_{k},\eta)}G(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})q^{\frac{1}{6}}(\eta)\varkappa(h\eta)\varkappa(h\tau_{q}(\omega_{k},\eta))r(\eta,\omega_{k})\sigma(\eta,\omega_{k})\\
\chi^{\flat}\Big(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\omega_{K_{\epsilon}}}\Big)q^{\frac{1}{6}}(\eta)\Big(r_{0}Ai(-\zeta(a,0,\eta,\omega_{k}))+iq^{-\frac{1}{6}}(\eta)r_{1}Ai^{\prime}(-\zeta(a,0,\eta,\omega_{k}))\Big)d\eta\,,

where
\zeta
is the phase introduced in Theorem
2.1
.

Proof.

Replacing
\hat{g}_{h,a,1}
given by Corollary
2.31
in formula (
2.31
) yields

K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{h,a,1})=\int e^{it\tau_{q}(\omega_{k},\eta)}G(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})\chi^{\#}(\omega_{k})q^{\frac{1}{6}}(\eta)\varkappa(h\eta)\varkappa(h\tau_{q}(\omega_{k},\eta))r(\eta,\omega_{k})\frac{\sqrt{L^{\prime}(\omega_{k})}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}I_{a}(\eta,\omega_{k})d\eta\,,

with
I_{a}(\eta,\omega_{k})
defined in (
2.115
). To compute
I_{a}
use (
2.3.1
) and
\overline{e(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})}=e^{-i|\eta|B_{0}}\overline{\tilde{e}(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})}

(4.3)

I_{a}=\int e^{-i(\tilde{y}\cdot(\eta-\tilde{\eta})+|\eta|B_{0}(\tilde{y},\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}))}\varkappa(h\tilde{\eta})\chi^{\flat}\Big(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\omega_{K_{\epsilon}}}\Big)e_{k}(a,\tilde{\eta})\left[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}\overline{\tilde{e}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\eta,\omega_{k})}e_{k}(\tilde{x},\tilde{\eta})d\tilde{x}\right]d\tilde{\eta}d\tilde{y}\,.

As in the proof of Lemma
2.28
, the bracket term in (
4.3
) behaves like a symbol. For small
a\lesssim h^{2/3-\epsilon}
and
k\leq K_{\epsilon}
,
e_{k}(a,\tilde{\eta})
can also be included in the symbol, and
with
\eta=\theta/h
and
\tilde{\eta}=\tilde{\theta}/h
, stationary phase applies in
\tilde{y},\tilde{\theta}
for the phase
-(\tilde{y}\cdot\theta+|\theta|B_{0}(\tilde{y},\vartheta))+\tilde{y}\cdot\tilde{\theta}
, with large parameter
\frac{1}{h}
and symbol

(4.4)

\frac{1}{h^{d-1}}\chi^{\flat}\Big(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\omega_{K_{\epsilon}}}\Big)\varkappa(\tilde{\theta})\chi_{0}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}q^{2/3}(\tilde{\theta}))e_{k}(a,\tilde{\theta}/h)\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\overline{\tilde{e}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\theta/h,\omega_{k})}e_{k}(\tilde{x},\tilde{\theta}/h)d\tilde{x}\Big)\,.

Stationary points are such that
\tilde{y}=0
,
\tilde{\theta}=\theta+|\theta|\partial_{\tilde{y}}B_{0}(\tilde{y},\vartheta)=\theta
(as
\partial_{y}B_{0}(0,\vartheta)=0
). All derivatives with respect to
\tilde{y}
land on
\overline{\tilde{e}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\theta/h,\omega_{k})}
and we can use Lemma
2.28
.
Derivatives with respect to
\tilde{\theta}
land either on cut-offs,
e_{k}(\tilde{x},\tilde{\theta}/h)
or
e_{k}(a,\tilde{\theta}/h)
: using (
2.107
) for
e_{k}
,

\Big|\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{\tilde{y}}\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{\tilde{\eta}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\overline{\tilde{e}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\eta,\omega_{k})}e_{k}(\tilde{x},\tilde{\eta})d\tilde{x}\Big)\Big|\leq\|\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{\tilde{y}}\tilde{e}(.,\omega_{k})\|_{L^{2}_{x\geq 0}}\|\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{\tilde{\eta}}e_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{x\geq 0}}\lesssim(\omega_{k}|\eta|^{1/3})^{|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|}\,.

As
|\eta|\sim 1/h
and
k\lesssim K_{\epsilon}<h^{-1/4+\epsilon}
we find
\omega_{k}|\eta|^{1/3}\leq h^{-1/2+2\epsilon/3}
. As one derivative on
e_{k}(a,\tilde{\theta}/h)
yields at most
\frac{a}{h}\sqrt{h^{2/3}\omega_{k}}
and we have
\frac{a}{h}\sqrt{h^{2/3}\omega_{k}}\leq h^{-1/12-2\epsilon/3}\ll h^{-1/2}
for
\epsilon<5/8
, stationary phase applies in
\tilde{y},\tilde{\theta}
. After stationary phase,
e_{k}(a,\tilde{\theta}/h)
transforms into a linear combination of
e_{k}
and its derivative : indeed, repeated derivatives in
\tilde{\theta}
lead to
Ai(aq^{1/3}(\tilde{\theta}/h)-\omega_{k})
and
Ai^{\prime}(aq^{1/3}(\tilde{\theta}/h)-\omega_{k})
, and we denote
r_{0},r_{1}
the corresponding asymptotic expansions;
r_{0},r_{1}
are functions of
(a,\theta/h,\omega_{k})
and
r_{0}
is elliptic with main contribution equal to
1
. Taking the difference between phase functions of
e_{k}(a,\tilde{\theta}/h)
and
e(a,0,\theta/h,\omega_{k})
yields
a\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)A_{\Gamma}(a,0,sq^{1/3}(\theta)/\tau_{q},\theta/\tau_{q})
, which behaves (at worst) like
a(s^{2}-h^{2/3}\omega_{k}q^{2/3}(\theta)+O(s^{3},s(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))
(where
s^{2}\lesssim h^{2/3}\omega_{k}
);
as
ah^{2/3}\omega_{k}\leq h^{7/6-\epsilon/3}
,
e^{\frac{i}{h}a\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)A_{\Gamma}(a,0,sq^{1/3}(\theta)/\tau_{q},\theta/\tau_{q})}
does not oscillate and can be brought into the symbol.
The
L^{2}_{x>0}
product in (
4.3
) becomes a new symbol
\sigma(\theta/h,\omega_{k})
, with main contribution
\sigma_{0}

\sigma_{0}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\overline{\tilde{e}(\tilde{x},0,\theta/h,\omega_{k})}e_{k}(\tilde{x},\theta/h)d\tilde{x}=h^{-2/3}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{i}{h}(-\tilde{s}^{3}/3-\tilde{s}(\tilde{x}q^{1/3}(\theta)-h^{2/3}\omega_{k})+s^{3}/3+s(\tilde{x}q^{1/3}(\theta)-h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))}\\
\times\frac{2\pi}{L^{\prime}(\omega_{k})}\frac{q^{1/3}(\theta)}{h^{2/3}}\tilde{p}_{h}(\tilde{x},\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\tilde{s},h/t)e^{-\frac{i}{h}\tilde{x}\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)A_{\Gamma}(\tilde{x},0,\sigma q^{1/3}(\theta)/\tau_{q},\theta/\tau_{q})}d\tilde{s}dsd\tilde{x},

for a symbol
\tilde{p}_{h}
obtained from
p_{h}
in (
2.22
). For
\tilde{x}\gtrsim h^{2/3}\omega_{k}
, repeated integrations by parts in
s
yield an
O(h^{\infty})
contribution. For
\tilde{x}\lesssim h^{2/3}\omega_{k}
, apply stationary phase in
s,\tilde{x}
: as
\tilde{x}q^{1/3}(\theta)=h^{2/3}\omega_{k}-s^{2}
and
sq^{1/3}(\theta)=\tilde{s}q^{1/3}(\theta)+\tau_{q}(A_{\Gamma}+\tilde{x}\partial_{\tilde{x}}A_{\Gamma})
, we are left with an integral in
\tilde{s}
with phase
e^{\frac{i}{h}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}-\tilde{s}^{2})q^{1/3}(-\theta)\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)A_{\Gamma}((h^{2/3}\omega_{k}-\tilde{s}^{2})q^{-1/3}(\theta),0,\tilde{s}q^{1/3}(\theta)/\tau_{q},\theta/\tau_{q})}
. One derivative with respect to
\theta
on this phase yields at most
\frac{1}{h}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2}\lesssim h^{4\epsilon/3}
for
k\leq K_{\epsilon}\leq h^{-1/4+\epsilon}
and therefore
\sigma_{0}
is of order
0
. Rewriting
K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{h,a,1})
after stationary phase, relabelling
r_{0}
and
r_{1}
, we obtain (
4.2
).
∎

Now we evaluate the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\mathcal{P}^{1}_{h,a}(t,.)
. If
d\geq 3
, we perform stationary phase in (
4.2
) w.r.t
\eta/|\eta|\in\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
:
\eta=\theta/h
,
\theta=|\theta|\vartheta
,
\theta\in\text{supp}(\varkappa)
, the phase of each
K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{h,a,1})(t,x,y)
is

t\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)+|\theta|(y\cdot\vartheta+B_{0}(y,\vartheta))+O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})=|\theta|(t+y\cdot\vartheta+B_{0}(y,\vartheta))+O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}),

where
O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})
contains contributions from
B_{\Gamma}-B_{0}
,
xA_{\Gamma}
and
aA_{\Gamma}
. Critical points
\vartheta_{\pm}
are given by (
3.58
) (with
\gamma=a
,
A=h^{2/3}\omega_{k}/(a|\theta|^{2/3})
,
S=s/(\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3})
,
\Sigma=\sigma/(\sqrt{a}|\theta|^{1/3}
)). As
\nabla^{2}_{\vartheta}(y\cdot\vartheta+B_{0}(y,\vartheta))\sim|y|
and
|y|\sim|t|
(Lemma
3.2
), stationary phase in
\vartheta
yields
({h}/{|\theta||y|})^{d-2}\leq C({h}/{|t|})^{d-2}
for
\vartheta
near
\vartheta_{\pm}
. For
\vartheta
outside a small neighborhood of
\vartheta_{\pm}
, we get
O(\frac{h}{t})^{\infty}
. Stationary phase yields new symbols as asymptotic expansions with small parameter
\frac{h}{t}
; the main contribution
K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{1,a,h})(t,x,y)
remains similar to (
4.2
), with a front factor
(h/t)^{(d-2)/2}
,
\eta=|\theta|\vartheta_{\pm}/h
and integration over
\eta
replaced by integration over
|\theta|
.

Lemma 4.3
.

(
[
9
, Lemma 3.5]
)
There exists
C_{0}
such that for
L\geq 1
,

(4.5)

\sup_{b\in{\mathbb{R}}}\Big(\sum_{1\leq k\leq L}\omega_{k}^{-1/2}Ai^{2}(b-\omega_{k})\Big)\leq C_{0}L^{1/3}\,,\quad\sup_{b\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}\Big(\sum_{1\leq k\leq L}\omega_{k}^{-1/2}h^{2/3}Ai^{\prime 2}(b-\omega_{k})\Big)\leq C_{0}h^{2/3}L\,.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz, dispersion for small
t
reduces to estimates like (
4.5
), as

(4.6)

\|\sum_{k=1}^{L}\frac{2\pi}{L^{\prime}(\omega_{k})}K_{\omega_{k}}(g_{h,a,1})(t,x,y)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim h^{-d}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}h^{1/3}\Big(L^{1/3}+h^{1/3}L^{2/3}+h^{2/3}L\Big),

where
h^{-(d-1)}(h/t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}
comes from stationary phase in
\vartheta
, while
h^{-2/3}=h^{-1}h^{1/3}
arises from
q^{1/3}(\theta/h)
and
L
-related terms come from (
4.5
).
Recall
h^{-2\epsilon}\leq K_{\epsilon}\leq h^{-1/4+\epsilon}
, hence
h^{\epsilon}\gg h^{2\epsilon}\geq\frac{1}{K_{\epsilon}}
. Let
L=h^{-\epsilon}
: for
t\leq h^{\epsilon}
we bound
L\leq\frac{1}{t}
in (
4.6
) and get (
4.1
) for the sum up to
h^{-\epsilon}
.

When
h^{\epsilon}\leq t
, we apply stationary phase in each oscillatory integral in the sum over
k
for
k\leq h^{-\epsilon}(\ll K_{\epsilon})
. Using Corollary
6.10
in the Appendix, we reformulate the phase
\psi
of Theorem
2.1
as

(4.7)

\psi(x,y,\theta,\omega_{k})=y\cdot\theta+\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)B_{\Gamma}(y,\theta/\tau_{q})+\Upsilon(x,y,\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k}).

Lemma 4.4
.

The stationary phase theorem with respect to
|\theta|
applies in (
4.2
) with large parameter
t\omega_{k}h^{-1/3}
,
with phase function
\phi_{h,k}(t,x,y,\theta)=t\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)+\psi(x,y,\theta,\omega_{k})
and with symbol

(4.8)

\sigma_{h,k}=\Big(p_{0}Ai(-\zeta(x,y,\theta/h,\omega_{k}))+ip_{1}Ai^{\prime}(-\zeta(x,y,\theta/h,\omega_{k}))\Big)q^{\frac{1}{3}}(\theta)\varkappa(\theta)\varkappa(\tau_{q}(h^{\frac{2}{3}}\omega_{k},\theta))\chi^{\flat}\Big(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\omega_{K_{\epsilon}}}\Big)\\
\times\sigma(\theta/h,\omega_{k})r(\theta/h,\omega_{k})\Big(r_{0}Ai(-\zeta(a,0,\theta/h,\omega_{k}))+iq^{-1/6}(\theta/h)r_{1}Ai(-\zeta(a,0,\theta/h,\omega_{k}))\Big)\,,

where
p_{0},p_{1}
were defined in (
2.8
) and
\theta=|\theta|\vartheta
with
\vartheta\in\{\vartheta_{\pm}\}
.

Proof.

Let
r=|\theta|
and recall
\vartheta\in\{\vartheta_{\pm}\}
(we already performed stationary phase in
\vartheta
). We have

(4.9)

\phi_{h,k}=t\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},\theta)+\psi(x,y,\theta,\omega_{k})=r\Big(t+y\cdot\vartheta+B_{0}(y,\vartheta)\Big)\\
+\frac{r^{1/3}}{2}h^{2/3}\omega_{k}q^{2/3}(\vartheta)\Big(t+B_{0}(y,\vartheta)-B_{2}(y,\vartheta)+O(|y|(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))\Big)+(rt)O((h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2})\\
-x\big(O(x)+O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})\big)-k_{1}\zeta\Big(\zeta+O(xh^{2/3}\omega_{k})+O((h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2})\Big),

where, in the second line,
O(|y|(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))
comes from
B_{\Gamma}-B_{0}-B_{2}
, and
(rt)O((h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2})
comes from
t\tau_{q}(h^{2/3}\omega_{k},r\vartheta)
and where the last line represents the contribution from
\Upsilon
as given in (
6.49
).

Here the critical points
\vartheta_{\pm}
are solutions to Equation (
3.58
), but with
\gamma A
replaced with
h^{2/3}\omega_{k}/r^{2/3}
in the whole equation. Then,
y\cdot\vartheta_{\pm}+B_{0}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})=\pm|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})|+O(a^{2})
,
a^{2}\ll h
. Moreover,
\partial_{r}\vartheta_{\pm}=O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})
(deriving (
3.58
); originally in (
3.58
) we made a suitable change of variables and got rid of
r=|\theta|
, which is no longer possible as we now work with fixed
\omega_{k}
).
A critical point with respect to
r
for
\phi_{h,k}
is such that

(4.10)

0=t\pm|y+\nabla B_{0}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})|+O(a^{2})+rO(|y|^{2})O(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})\\
{}+\frac{2}{3}r^{-2/3}h^{2/3}\omega_{k}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{\pm})\Big(t+B_{0}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})-B_{2}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})+O(|y|(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))\Big)+tO((h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2})\\
{}-\partial_{r}k_{1}\zeta^{2}(x,y,\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k})+O(x^{2},x(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))\,,

where the last line is
\partial_{r}\Upsilon
, whose main contribution is
-\partial_{r}k_{1}\zeta^{2}(x,y,\theta,\omega_{k})
: as
h^{2/3}\zeta(x,y,\theta/h,\omega_{k})=\zeta(x,y,\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k})=h^{2/3}\omega_{k}-xq^{1/3}(\theta)e_{0}(x,y,\vartheta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k}/|\theta|)
,
\partial_{r}\zeta
yields a factor
x
, which puts everything else in
O(x^{2},x(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))
. Moreover, set
t(>h)>0
as usual, only
\vartheta_{-}
is to be kept (at
\vartheta_{+}
the phase will be non-stationary).
As
B_{0}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})-B_{2}(y,\vartheta_{\pm})=O(|y|^{2})=O(t^{2})
and
x\lesssim h^{2/3-\epsilon}
, the main contribution of
\partial^{2}_{r}\phi_{h,k}
comes from first terms on the second and last lines of (
4.10
)

(4.11)

-\frac{4}{9}r^{-5/3}h^{2/3}\omega_{k}q^{2/3}(\vartheta_{\pm})t\Big(1+O(|y|^{2}/t)+O(|y|/t)\times(h^{2/3}\omega_{k}))+O((h^{2/3}\omega_{k})^{2})\Big)\\
-\partial^{2}k_{1}\zeta^{2}(x,y,\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k})+O(x^{2},x(h^{2/3}\omega_{k})).

Observe that
h^{2/3}\omega_{k}t\gg\zeta^{2}(x,y,\theta,h^{2/3}\omega_{k})
for all
t>h^{\epsilon}
: indeed,
h^{\epsilon}\gg h^{2/3}\omega_{k}
, which holds true for all
\omega_{k}\leq\omega_{K_{\epsilon}}\leq h^{-1/6+2\epsilon/3}
. At the stationary point satisfying (
4.10
),
|y|
behaves like
t
, and therefore
\partial_{r}^{2}\phi_{h,k}
behaves like
th^{2/3}\omega_{k}
there.
We are left to prove that stationary phase indeed applies with large parameter
(th^{2/3}\omega_{k})/h\sim t\omega_{k}h^{-1/3}
, for
h^{\epsilon}\lesssim t
and symbol
\sigma_{h,k}
from (
4.8
).
Computing
\partial_{r}^{2}\sigma_{h,k}
yields at most
\omega_{k}^{11/4}
which occurs when
\zeta(x,y,\theta/h,\omega_{k})
is large,
\zeta(a,0,\theta/h,\omega_{k})
is bounded and when both derivatives fall on of the first Airy: hence, it suffices to check
\frac{h^{1/3}}{t\omega_{k}}\omega_{k}^{11/4}\ll 1
for
t\geq h^{\epsilon}
,
uniformly in
k\lesssim h^{-\epsilon}
: this does hold as long as
\epsilon<2/13
and we already set
\epsilon<1/12
. ∎

We then sum up to
k\leq h^{-\epsilon}
, for
t\geq h^{\epsilon}
and
\epsilon<1/12
, with
L^{\prime}(\omega_{k})\sim 2\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}
:

(4.12)

\|\mathcal{P}^{1}_{h,a}(t,x,y)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{h^{1/3}}{h^{d}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\sum_{k\lesssim h^{-\epsilon}}\frac{1}{L^{\prime}(\omega_{k})}\Big(\frac{h^{1/3}}{t\omega_{k}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{-\frac{\epsilon}{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\Big(\frac{h}{t}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\,.

It remains to deal with
h^{-\epsilon}\leq k\leq K_{\epsilon}
and
t\in(h,T_{0}]
. Taking
L=K_{\epsilon}
in (
4.6
), we obtain (
4.1
) exactly as before for all
t\leq K_{\epsilon}^{-1}
(up to
k\leq K_{\epsilon}
). Hence we are left with
t\geq K_{\epsilon}^{-1}
and
h^{-\epsilon}\leq k\lesssim K_{\epsilon}
. It suffices to consider
0\leq x,a\lesssim h^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
(as for
x\geq h^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
, the arguments for the transverse case
a>h^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
apply).
For small values of both
x,a
,
|\zeta(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})|\geq\omega_{k}/2
and
|\zeta(a,0,\eta,\omega_{k})|\geq\omega_{k}/2
(
k\geq h^{-\epsilon}
is large so
\omega_{k}\geq h^{-2\epsilon/3}\gg h^{-\epsilon}\gtrsim a/h^{2/3}
). We can write
Ai(-\zeta)=\sum_{\pm}A_{\pm}(\zeta)
which give rise to factors
\zeta(x,y,\eta,\omega_{k})^{-1/4}\zeta(a,0,\eta,\omega_{k})^{-1/4}
